【译】……不再像从前那样让我兴奋了。如果说得通的话——我的人生不是围绕钱运转的。我的人生靠什么运转？我必须工作。我 65 岁了。人们问：「你会退休吗？」我有相当不错的退休金。我是百分之百伤残的退伍军人，也就是说，我的医疗全包，什么都有。那我还能干什么？你是了解我的：你觉得我坐得下来什么都不干吗？——是啊。——个屁，绝无他妈的可能（ain't a fucking chance in hell）。你是了解我的。就是这样，听我说，我对人生是这么看的——我一直说人生短暂、要活出自己的人生，我是认真的。无论是什么，每当我看到有人为了别人而压抑自己喜欢做的事，我真的、真的为他们感到难过。——罗布·纳尔逊（Rob Nelson），Rockstar North 的负责人之一，说在游戏里几乎任何东西都能抢，地图上任何一家店都行。我对制作这些电子游戏需要什么非常着迷，其中有大量的取舍要做。「什么都能抢」在执行上是极难的。——基本上，你可以走进一家 7-Eleven，对吧？抓几件东西。——咱们先暂停一下这个话题。——怎么了？——谁会去抢 7-Eleven 啊？只有傻子（moron）才会抢 7-Eleven。——我得……好吧，你是想把这个剪进去：7-Eleven 是留给傻子抢的。——好，我明白你的意思了。为什么？——因为问题不在于「在哪儿抢」，而在于钱在哪儿。——是，是。——我想听你说过：你从来不碰偷画那一类的事。——我本来是可以（偷画）的。这里有个故事。我拥有一家保安公司，一百来号人。有个给我干活的家伙打电话给我——首先，我雇他是因为他是个不省心的人，你知道，有前科之类的，但他当保安还行，我让他过了关，我不在乎。他去了南边。他说：「拉里，我在……

[2280s - 2400s]

Miami Convention Center, and it's an art show. So he calls me up and he says, "Hey, Larry, he goes, 'There's Picasso's, there's Rembrandts.' Listen, I'm guarding it. You want it? I'll let you in. Literally, I said, 'Hold on, I'll call you tomorrow.' I made a few phone calls to New York, Lex. I couldn't get two cents on a dollar. I don't care about pictures worth a million dollars, and you're going to get ten grand. What am I going to do with that? So that's the that's the fence job. Fence. You couldn't. We couldn't fence it. What was uh? What what could you get on jewelry? Jewelry. I was getting twenty five, thirty percent, depending on what it is. Watches were set price. Every standard Rolex, two grand. Standard, you know, Oyster Perpetual, the bullshits that are four grand, five grand. I don't even know what they are today. Maybe six grand. They were about four grand back then. Two grand, bang! I robbed fifty of them. Boom, hundred k. Not a question. Put them aside. Now look, and now I'm a Apple Watch. How the times have changed. Yes, so it is. Because you mentioned it was the eighties. What do you think about? I don't think I've ever talked to you about Scarface. What do you think about Scarface? The the movie is one of those legendary portrayals. What? How? What? Why am I so in this? Watch this. Okay. Yeah. I was the first boat. I'm retired Coast Guard. I was the first boat on sea for the Marielitos, the Marielito boat lift. That's when all the Cubans came here. Can you explain that boat? Okay, Mariel. They they call it the Mariel boat lift. Cuba let out in this is 1980. Cuba let all its prisoners out of the prisons, and there was a mass exodus of Cuba to the United States. I was in the Coast Guard, so I was the first boat the Coast Guard sent there, and we were out in the ocean picking up dead.

【译】……迈阿密会议中心（Miami Convention Center）干活，那儿在办一场艺术展。」他打电话给我说：「嘿，拉里，」他说，「有毕加索（Picasso），有伦勃朗（Rembrandt）。听着，看守的人是我。你想要吗？我放你进来。」我当时真的说了：「等一下，我明天给你回电话。」我给纽约打了几个电话，莱克斯。一美元的画，我连两美分都换不到。那些画值不值一百万美元我根本不在乎——你到头只能到手一万块，我要那一万块干什么？所以这就是销赃（fence）的问题。——销赃。——你们销不掉。我们销不掉。——那……珠宝你们能拿到多少？——珠宝，我能拿两成半到三成，看具体是什么货。手表是按死价算的：每块标准劳力士，2000 美元；标准的，你知道，Oyster Perpetual 那类「破玩意儿」，4000、5000 美元——我都不知道今天什么价，也许 6000 了。那时候一块大约 4000。2000 一块，「砰」！我抢过 50 块，轰，十万美元到手，毫无悬念，往旁边一搁。再看看现在——我现在戴的是 Apple Watch。时代真是变了。——确实是。——对了，你提到那是八十年代——我好像从没跟你聊过《疤面煞星》（Scarface）。你觉得《疤面煞星》怎么样？那部电影可是那种传奇级的刻画。什么？怎么说？我怎么看？……为什么我陷得这么深？看看这个。好。——我那趟是第一条船。我是退役的海岸警卫队（Coast Guard）。马列尔人（Marielitos）渡海潮、也就是马列尔渡船潮（Marielito boat lift）的时候，我是海上的第一条（救援）船。那就是古巴人成群来到美国的时期。——你能讲讲那次渡海吗？——好，马列尔。他们管它叫马列尔渡船潮。这是 1980 年，古巴把监狱里的犯人全放了出来，出现了古巴向美国的大规模出逃。我当时在海岸警卫队，我们是海岸警卫队派去的第一条船，我们就在大海中间打捞……

[2400s - 2520s]

Bodies. It was really, really traumatic because I have such heart for Cuban people because I remember a boat, about a thirty-five, forty-foot boat with fifty-five gallon gas cans blow up. We saw it in the distance, and then we're racing towards that thing, and there's bodies, some screaming, some floating. Do you do you go by this one, and he's still alive? And it was we we stacked the bodies in the back of the Coast Guard boat. I don't know what we how we were dealing with that as young kids back then, just as young kids seeing so much death, so much death. It was really that's why I'm really for and a big supporter of our military, and but we need to help them. They come back from Afghanistan, killing people. You that we you know they're seeing some stuff. I know some of them, and I talk to them a lot, and we need to help them because you can't. They're too young to cope with. They think they can, they can't. I know. I maybe that's listen. I was crazy for a lot of reasons, but going back to Scarface, that is what that movie. They had that stuff under the bridges. They didn't know what to do with the people, so they started shipping them. See, most of them came into Key West. They didn't come into. That's where I was, Key West. I was stationed in Cortez, Florida, which is on the west. It's another thing, you know, Sunshine Skyway Bridge collapse. I was the first boat there, and so when you know when the Marielito had, we were sent right down there because we're close. We're Cortez, which is in Bradenton, Sarasota area. So we went around the tip. We went down to Cuba. And Al Pacino, the Scarface character, was on. Is the criminal on that boat from Marielitos? Yes, that's what he was. And they put those people in under the. That's all real. They put them under the bridge. They didn't know what to do with them. They were making temporary cities for people. So I think Scarface is.

【译】……尸体。那真的、真的很创伤，因为我对古巴民众有极深的感情。因为我记得有一条船，大约 35 到 40 英尺的船，绑着 55 加仑的汽油桶，爆炸了。我们远远看见它，然后全速朝那儿冲，到处是尸体：有的在尖叫，有的漂在水上。你从这一具旁边划过——他还活着。我们就这样把尸体一具具码在海警船的船尾。我不知道当年作为一群年轻人，我们是怎么消化这一切的；就那么年轻的孩子，见到那么多的死亡，那么多死亡。真的——这也是为什么我真心支持我们的军队，是军队的坚定支持者。但我们需要帮他们。他们从阿富汗回来，杀过人。他们见过一些东西。我认识其中一些人，常跟他们谈，我们必须帮他们，因为他们扛不住——太年轻了。他们以为自己扛得住，其实扛不住。我知道。也许正因如此……听我说，我当年疯，有很多原因。但回到《疤面煞星》——那部电影拍的（就是原型）。他们把那些东西安排在桥底下。政府不知道拿这些人怎么办，就开始转运他们。你看，他们大多是从基韦斯特（Key West）进来的，不是从……我就在那儿，基韦斯特。我驻扎在佛州的科尔特斯（Cortez），在西边。还有一件事——阳光天桥（Sunshine Skyway Bridge）坍塌，我是到场的第一条船。而马列尔渡海潮开始时，我们因为离得近，立刻就被派了过去。我们驻在科尔特斯，就在布雷登顿（Bradenton）、萨拉索塔（Sarasota）那一带。于是我们绕过（半岛的）尖端，一路南下到了古巴。而阿尔·帕西诺（Al Pacino），《疤面煞星》的那个角色，就在船上。——他是马列尔渡海潮船上来的那个罪犯？——对，他就是。他们把那些人安置在……——这些都是真的。他们把人安置在桥底下，不知道拿他们怎么办，就给他们搭起了临时之城。所以我觉得《疤面煞星》……

[2520s - 2640s]

So one of the most culture-defining portrayals of Miami and of the criminal life. I mean, it's really it's all of it together, and also the the rise coming from nothing. It's in some sense, it's it's kind of like weird, dark, caricatured view of the American dream of in a self reliant way making the most of this of a hard situation you're in and rising to the very top. Did you know how they made that? I do. What happened with that is Al Pacino pushed that. They didn't want to make that, and he kept coming back to that for years. I I just just not long ago was listening to a show on that and how and why he wanted to do it, and because of that, because it was so dark, like you said, it was so rough. Did they want that? You got to remember how long ago that was, and that movie he pushed because he believed in it because he met some people who were from that the real people, and it is so so on point, and it was on point because it showed Miami the money, the guy walking in with bags of money to a banker that's corrupt, and everybody's putting you know like remember the couple scenes the guys are literally bringing duffel bags. So there's some aspects to that movie that's hundred percent true. Hundred percent true. Listen, when I say I'm not the only one, everybody carried money in their trunk to corrupt the cops. Everybody, it was it was the Wild West if you want to consider Wild West, and South Beach was like really the really hot the clubs the you know everything was just rocking, and it was not fancy like it is today either. Yeah, it was more Art Deco. That's why I I you know I remember more of the all the colors the stuff like that. Now it is so corporate and so it's still Miami. It's

【译】……是对迈阿密和犯罪生活最具文化定义性的刻画之一。我是说，真的是所有元素都聚在了一起，还有那种从一无所有起家的崛起。某种意义上，它像是一种怪异、黑暗、漫画化的美国梦图景：靠自己、把你身处的不利处境榨到极致，一路爬到最顶端。——你知道他们是怎么把这部片子拍出来的吗？——我知道。事情是这样的：是阿尔·帕西诺力推的。他们本来不想拍，而他年复一年地回头死磕这个项目。就在不久前，我还听了一档讲这件事的节目，讲他为什么、怎么想拍它。也正因为这样——因为它太黑暗了，就像你说的，太粗粝了。他们想看那种东西吗？你要记得那是多少年前的事了。那部片子是他力推的，因为他信它，因为他见过一些真正来自那个世界的人——真实的人。而它拍得那么、那么到位。它之所以到位，是因为它展示了迈阿密、展示了钱——一个人拎着一袋袋钱走进去、走向一个腐败银行家——你还记得那几场戏吗，那些人是真的拖着行李袋进去的。所以那部电影里有某些层面是百分之百真实的。——百分之百真实。听我说，当我说不只是我一个人——当年每个人后备箱里都放着用来打点警察的现金。每一个人。那完全是「狂野西部」（Wild West），如果你想用狂野西部来形容的话。而南海滩（South Beach）真是热得发烫——夜店啊，你知道，一切都火爆得不行，而且那时候也不像今天这么精致。——对，那时候更有装饰艺术（Art Deco）的味道。这也是为什么我印象更深的是那些色彩之类的东西。现在一切都太企业化了，不过它仍然是迈阿密。它……

[2640s - 2760s]

You'll still see the old Cubans. I'm gonna take you to some areas where you'll see the Cubans sitting on it on the porches. I'm gonna take you some places where the the Cuban coffee shops. Yeah, definitely, you're gonna love those. Okay, what is there's there's so many awesome things about Cuban culture, but the coffee is well, no, it's like Italian. This, but I love espresso. I'm an espresso. But they drink it like at night. It's crazy. Drink it all day. We used to drink a pot of that. Yeah. At nine at night, and you know people say you can't go to bed. When I want to go to bed, yeah, I go to bed. I go to bed. Yeah, yeah, I'm the same. Did you ever hear about the Simas Kudirka incident? Gonna look this one. People, yeah, Simas Kudirka, the Lithuanian seaman who attempted defection because a major cold. He jumped on a Coast Guard. Controversy: The Soviet Union convicted him of treason and sentenced him to ten years in prison, but investigators subsequently established a claim to U.S. citizenship through his American-born mother. He was released in that. No, just say right here, seventy-eight. U.S. Coast Guard superiors ordered that he be returned. And so what happened was, in the Coast Guard, we were briefed because I was in Alaska during the Cold War, and I used to bring, I used to board Russian ships. I love Russian people. I used to drink with them on the boat. People, you know, I love that people think of people differently. I'll tell you why. We used to go board big fishing factory ships from Russia. It's called the 200 miles fisheries conservation zone. The United States has, so we used to board Japanese, Maru, and Russian all these boats. So I remember I was a boat captain of the small boat. Okay, so I would bring the people over. I brought the people over, our crew, and then we'd go on the boat and we'd literally count fish. They count it by tons. They know the square footages of a hole, and we'd find them a million dollars. So after that incident, we were always briefed that if you get.

【译】……你仍然会看到老一辈的古巴人。我会带你去一些街区，在那儿你会看到古巴人坐在门廊上。我会带你去那些古巴咖啡馆。——好，一定，你肯定会喜欢的。——好。古巴文化有那么多美妙的东西，但咖啡嘛——不，它其实更像意大利（咖啡）。不过我爱浓缩咖啡（espresso），我是浓缩咖啡派的。可他们连晚上都喝，这就疯了。他们整天喝。我们过去一整壶一整壶地喝，晚上九点还在喝，人们总说「这样你没法睡觉」。我想睡的时候——是啊，我倒头就睡。——是，我也一样。——你听说过西蒙纳斯·库迪尔卡（Simas Kudirka）事件吗？——我来查一下这个。是这样：西蒙纳斯·库迪尔卡，立陶宛海员，在一次重大冷战（事件）中企图叛逃。他跳上了一艘海岸警卫队的船。争议点在于：苏联以叛国罪判处他十年监禁，但调查人员后来确认，他可以通过他美国出生的母亲主张美国公民身份。他随后获释。——不对，就看这里：七八年——美国海岸警卫队的上级下令把他遣返。于是事情是这样：在海警队里，我们会接受简报，因为我冷战期间驻扎在阿拉斯加，我常常登俄国船。我喜爱俄国民众。我曾在船上和他们喝酒。人们啊，你知道，我喜欢让人们不带成见地看待彼此。我告诉你为什么。我们过去常登俄国的大型渔业加工船。有个东西叫 200 海里渔业养护区（200 miles fisheries conservation zone），是美国设立的，所以我们常登日本船、丸（Maru）号、俄国船，各种船都登。我记得我当时是小艇艇长。好，我负责把人送过去——把我们的人送过去，然后我们上船，真的就是数鱼。他们按吨报数，他们知道船舱的平方英尺面积，我们会查出他们上百万美元（的超捕渔获）。所以在那次事件之后，我们总是被简告：一旦你遇到……

[2760s - 2880s]

Anybody Russian, if they jump in the water, anything, you have to bring them back to the Coast Guard ship, and then it becomes a State Department issue. We had to bring it; we could not give them back, no matter what. So we get on the Russian ships. Now, here is where I always say we, United States, and Russia, all of that brainwashing, all of that political propaganda, because it's all propaganda. We used to how the evil empire, the Russians, and they hear the Americas, or whatever it is. Such bullshit. Because I'd I'd go on the boats, and we'd be on those boats for hours. Because the crews are doing everything. I didn't do anything. I was the boat captain. I I remember sitting drinking with Russian guys on the boat. Boy, they could drink, man. I got so much fun drinking with them and hanging with them, and they just another guy would come in. They drink, nice guys. Yeah, just drinking vodka, vodka right out of a bottle. Could you could you keep up? Or yeah, I was a pretty good party. Let me just leave it that. Yeah, I actually could. All right, I actually could, and it was funny because I was worried about getting caught by by our ship, you know. But no, you know it was funny because in nineteen seventy nine, yeah, I was a Coast Guard station Cortez, smoking weed. We got caught. Everybody, nothing happened. They gave us what they call a page seven, meant nothing. Now, if you got caught drinking in the Coast Guard, you get kicked out. I mean, just I don't know if that's good or bad, and I think I liked our way better because, like me, yeah, you can when you create rules. T here is pros and cons. The the negative of that is you lose some of the humanity, as you talked. That's the positive is like you know, t here is bad people in all professions, and if you don't have enough rules, and the bad people can do bad, and the corruption, all this kind of stuff. So it's it's a double edged sword. You lose the humanity. I am for the humanity. I am sorry, I am less rules and more common sense. We worked hard.

【译】……任何俄国人——不管他们跳进水里还是怎样——都必须带回海警船，然后事情就交给国务院处理了。我们必须把人带回来，无论如何都不能把人交回去。于是我们登上俄国船。而这里就是我一直说的：我们美国和俄国之间的那一套洗脑、那一套政治宣传——因为那全是宣传。我们这边讲「邪恶帝国」俄国人，他们那边听的则是「美国人如何如何」。全是屁话。因为我会上那些船，一待就是几个小时。活儿都是船员在干，我什么也不用干，我是艇长嘛。我记得坐在船上和俄国人喝酒。老天，那帮人是真能喝。跟他们喝酒、跟他们厮混，太开心了，一会儿又进来一个人——喝，都是好人。对，就那么对着瓶子灌伏特加。——你跟得上他们的量吗？——呃，我算是个挺能玩的。这么说吧。——好。——其实我跟得上。行，我真的跟得上。而且好笑的是，我还担心被我们自己的军舰抓到，你知道吧。不过，说件好玩的事：1979 年，对，我在海岸警卫队科尔特斯站（Cortez）服役，抽大麻，被逮到了。所有人（一起被逮），结果什么都没发生。他们给了我们一个所谓的「page seven」（第 7 页处分），毫无意义。可在海警队，你要是被抓到喝酒，是要被开除的。我都不知道这算好还是坏，反正我觉得我们那个年代的方式更好——就像我这样的。是——你一旦制定规则——凡事就各有利弊。坏处是你会失去一部分人性，就像你说的；好处是：每个行业都有坏人，如果规则不够多，坏人就能作恶，腐败啊这一类的东西就会滋生。所以是把双刃剑：你失去了人性。我站在人性这一边，抱歉，我支持少一点规则、多一点常识。我们那会儿干活拼命……

[2880s - 3000s]

You know, Lex, I can honestly say I love the Coast Guard, and I work. I love it, man. I I drink coffee and and do the boats, you know, because I had a lot of guys, I had sixty guys working for me on the ship, and but we ran the deck, they call it, like the boats operations up and down davits, anything to do with anything like that. And I used to put out the mess cooks. I used to do all that. I I was charged all that, and I love the work. You work hard, but you played hard. And I am for the humanity part. I really believe this this country's got too many laws, and I think that's really hurt us. I think we're becoming very robotic, if you want to call it, instead of being more human and more compassionate, more. I listen when I get to know people, and I I will fight for them. I will go to war for them, and even with the laws. That's why I went away because my word was what I I grew up on. And do I did I know I was doing wrong? Of course. Do I think this should be a law against robbing? Yes, of course. I'm not a nut, but I think when I say laws is everything a cop pulls you over for is mostly bullshit. It's it's it's it's a money grab. It's it's taxing us. It's what it's doing, and it's wrong. And you're taxing the wrong people. You're taxing the people who can't afford it. You're not taxing the rich guy who doesn't drive. He's got somebody driving for him. You're not taxing him. You're taxing that poor guy that can't make his. And in that kind of system, it's important not to lose your humanity. Oh, that's where I think cops lose the the battle. Listen, if you just thought of every one of those people as your neighbor, would you give them a ticket? I hope not. Listen, the guy's going a hundred miles an hour, is going to kill a kid in the street. I even say, yes. I even you know I speak at a lot of police things. I was in Dallas, and I spoke at the you know like this International Association of Chiefs of Police.

【译】你知道吗，莱克斯，我可以诚实地说：我爱海岸警卫队，我在那儿干活——我爱那份工作。喝着咖啡、操持船艇，你知道，我手底下有很多人，船上有 60 个人为我干活。我们负责甲板运转，他们管那叫 run the deck——就是吊柱（davit）上上下下的小艇作业，凡是跟这类事相关的都算。我还负责安排帮厨（mess cook）上岗，这些都归我管，而我爱那份工作。拼命干活，也拼命地玩。而我站在「人性」这一边。我真心相信，这个国家的法律太多了，我认为这真的伤害了我们。我觉得我们正在变得非常机械化——如果你想这么形容——而不是更有人性、更有同情心。当我认识了一个人，我会倾听，我会为他们而战，为他们拼到底，哪怕是与法律对抗。我之所以蹲了监狱，就是因为我的承诺是我从小长大的根基。那我知不知道自己在做错事？当然知道。我认不认为应该有禁止抢劫的法律？当然认为，我又不是疯子。但我说的「法律太多」是指：警察把你拦下来开罚单的那些事，大多都是屁事，是敛财（money grab），是在向我们征税——它干的就是这个，而这是错的。而且你征的是不该征的人：你征的是那些交不起的人。你没有征那个自己不开车的富人——他有人替他开车——你没征到他，你征的是那个挣不出罚款的穷人。而在那样一种体制里，重要的是别丢了你的人性。——哦，这正是我认为警察输掉的那场「战斗」。听着，只要你把那些人中的每一个都当成你的邻居，你还会给他们开罚单吗？我希望不会。当然，那种开到时速一百英里、要撞死街上孩子的家伙，这种我照样说该罚。还有，你知道，我常在警察界的活动上演讲。我去过达拉斯，在……就是那个国际警察局长协会（International Association of Chiefs of Police）……

[3000s - 3120s]

It goes to the IACP. I'm invited to speak to the ethics committee. I'm teaching ethics to the world. Now this is the International Society. There were chiefs from China, Germany, Russia. They were all there, and I'm in the room and I speak. And when I say the laws have changed to the point of, how many times do we have to get, cost our citizens more and more money and more and more headaches? Because what happens when we do that? Us against them. People hate the cops. I don't like that. When people hate the cops, I understand it. I don't like it. But who has to change that? The cops. So if you get a law enforcement leader who is community minded, here's why. I got a badge. I'm an honorary police officer from the city of Lake Saint Louis, and to this day, the chief of police. Well, he retired. The new chief I know as well. He was the assistant chief. His name is Christy Giuseppe, Lake Saint Louis, one of best police chiefs I know. Here's why. Not because he gave me a badge. He didn't even give me the badge. His mentor gave me the badge, and his mentor was a police chief for 25 years. The average duration of a police chief is three years. They get fired. They do something. They three years. The council gets rid of them. This guy's 25 years. That says it all. Well, when I was given my police badge, the people in that town, even the criminals, did not hate him, because he knew how to talk to people. He knew how to help people. He would not arrest a kid and try to not put something on his record and give him that break. And he and he justified and he was right. Forget the law. Right.

【译】事情还传到了国际警察局长协会（IACP）那里。我受邀在伦理委员会演讲，等于在给全世界讲授伦理。这是个国际协会，有来自中国、德国、俄国的警察局长，全都在场，我在会场里发言。我说到法律已经变本加厉：我们还要让公民花越来越多的钱、受越来越多的罪到什么时候？因为这样做的后果是什么？"我们对抗他们"（us against them），民众恨警察。我不喜欢这样。民众恨警察时，我理解，但我不喜欢。那谁得去改变这一点？警察。所以如果有一位有社区意识的执法领袖——原因在这儿：我有一枚徽章，我是圣路易斯湖市（Lake Saint Louis）的名誉警察，直到今天……那位警察局长——嗯，他退休了——新局长我也认识，他原来是副手，名叫 Christy Giuseppe，圣路易斯湖市的，是我认识的最优秀的警察局长之一。原因是：不是因为他给了我徽章——徽章甚至不是他给的，是他的导师给我的，而他导师当了25年警察局长。警察局长的平均任期是三年：他们被解雇、出点事，三年议会就把他们弄走。这人干了25年，这说明一切。我被授予警察徽章时，那个镇上的人，连罪犯都不恨他，因为他懂怎么跟人说话，懂怎么帮人。他不会去逮捕一个孩子再往孩子档案上记一笔，他会给那孩子一次机会。他这么做是有道理的，他是对的。别管法律条文，对吧。

[3120s - 3240s]

Now we're trying to save a life, and we don't get that. We got guys that get in office because they want to shoot. I can't stand when they say, "Well, head a SWAT, head it." That shows me you only know one way. I tell you another thing about you. Want stories? Yeah, I was a young captain on the boat, riding the boat, collecting people. In the distance, we see white. Now we're about fifteen miles off Cuba. Now every country is respected. Three miles from the coast of a country is their waters. It's called territorial waters. But Russia could put a sub five miles off our coast. Now, twelve miles out is what they call customs waters. Okay, that's a little bit more in depth. Where any American boat, anything that they that's monitored, that's like our waters too. But but technically, they could do that. Most countries respect customs waters twelve miles. So we're fifteen miles out. We were told, "Don't go in inside. Don't go near that marker." We we were told twelve miles. No, we were told stay fifteen. So we were at fifteen miles, and we make sure you know we got radars and all that, even back then. But I learned how to be a captain back when we had to do sat nav. We had to do celestial navigation, which is oh my god with a sextant. I don't. That's where oh yeah, do the stars and all that. It's like analog, not digital. What that's before that? You're talking about you're talking about LORAN A, LORAN C. That's before even satellite. I mean, this is way we had to do with a compass, just a compass. You had enough. Listen, people don't know how hard that is. You go out to rescue somebody who's eighty miles out off the coast. If you're off by a degree, by the time you get there, you're you won't find them. You're twenty miles off because this current set drift all that. Well, you had to know that. Anyway, we're twelve. We're fifteen miles off. See a thing coming. Holy shit! Was a Cuban gunboat.

【译】现在我们在救人命，却得不到这种理解。有些人进办公室就是想开枪。我最受不了有人说"上特警（SWAT），派特警去"。那说明你只会一种手段。再跟你说件关于我的事。想听故事？好。我那时是船上年轻的船长，驾着船，一路上收容（海上的人）。远处我们看到白色浪花。我们当时在古巴外海约十五英里。每个国家都受到尊重：离一国海岸三英里之内是其水域，叫领海（territorial waters）。但俄国可以把潜艇停在我们海岸五英里外。再往外十二英里是所谓关税水域（customs waters）——这个就更深入一点：任何美国船只、任何受监控的对象，那也等同于我们的水域。但严格说，他们技术上可以那么做。多数国家尊重十二英里的关税水域。所以我们在十五英里外。我们接到命令："别进去，别靠近那个标记。"我们被告知……十二英里？不，是被告知保持在十五英里。我们就待在十五英里处，而且确保——你知道的，我们有雷达之类的东西，即便在那个年代。但我学当船长那会儿还得做卫星导航，还得做天文导航——天哪，用六分仪（sextant），靠星星定位那一套。那是模拟时代的活儿，不是数字的。在那之前呢？你说到的可是 LORAN A、LORAN C（远程无线电导航系统），那还在卫星之前。我是说，更早的时候我们只能靠罗盘，就一个罗盘，也够用。听着，人们不知道那有多难。你去救一个在离岸八十英里外的人，如果你偏了一度，等你到那儿就找不到他们了——会差出二十英里，因为洋流、风压差这些。这些你都必须懂。总之，我们在……我们在十五英里外，看见一个东西开过来。我靠！是一艘古巴炮艇。

[3240s - 3360s]

Hundred foot. We're kids. Grab the mic, Mayday, Mayday, Coast Guard. You know, I said we'd be an attack. Yeah, we didn't want to be an attack, but no, they were coming at us, so we didn't know what to do. And all we hear a calm, older voice, "Coast Guard, come dead in the water. Make no overt moves. Helps underway." Sorry, man. I'm not kidding you, Lex. A minute before he even got to us, we didn't we didn't see it. We heard it. F-16s come off the deck. Two of them, hundred feet off the water. One goes this way up. One goes right towards it. Guys from the big Coast Guard formation, you hear? This is whoever he's his callsign. I'm above you. You're safe. Don't worry, we got you. Pass on to Coast Guard Cutter Dallas. The Dallas was the on scene commander, which is another one I was on type I was on, but I was in Hawaii on the Jarvis. It's the Dallas. So he's up above us. This other boat we're watching, fucking the the plane, fucking fucking with this Cuban gumbo. Cuban gumbo turns. We see in the distance white. What the fuck is that? A Navy hydrofoil comes flying by us at sixty miles an hour. This is the water, a hundred foot boat with fucking hours now with rockets and shit. Now this gumbo before they turned, they were fuck hundred yard, two hundred two hundred yards maybe. So like, but now like we're just watching it and listen. I'm we're in the cabin, but I'm listening to radio, of course. And the voices were always older. I know I'll never forget that and calm, and they were trying to calm us. So all of a sudden that goes. Now that's fucking with the cop. It's literally circling. Then.

【译】一百英尺（约三十米）长。我们还是些孩子。抓起话筒：Mayday、Mayday，呼叫海岸警卫队。我说我们要遭攻击了。是啊，我们并不想被攻击，但他们朝我们冲过来，我们不知道该怎么办。然后我们只听见一个沉稳的、年长的声音："遇险船，停车（dead in the water），不要做任何多余动作，救援已在途中。"真的，老兄，我不骗你，莱克斯。他到我们这儿的前一分钟，我们还没看见，先听见了：F-16 从甲板上起飞，两架，离水面一百英尺，一架这样拉起来，一架直冲那艘船。大编队海岸警卫队的人，你听到了吧？这是某人的呼号（callsign）："我在你上方，你安全了，别担心，我们罩着你。转告海岸警卫队'达拉斯号'巡逻舰（Cutter Dallas）。"达拉斯号是现场指挥舰，也是我后来服役过的同型舰——不过我那时在夏威夷的"贾维斯号"（Jarvis）上。是达拉斯号。他在我们上方。我们看着那艘船，飞机在那儿折腾这艘古巴炮艇（gumbo，他口误指 gunboat）。古巴炮艇转向。远处我们又看到白色。那他妈是什么？一艘海军水翼艇以六十英里时速从我们身边飞驰而过。在水面上，一百英尺的船，装着火箭什么玩意的。那艘炮艇转向之前，大概离我们一两百码吧。就这样，我们在旁边看着、听着——我们在舱里，当然我一直在听电台。那些声音永远是年长的人，我永远忘不了：很沉稳，他们在努力让我们镇定下来。突然之间局势变了。那是在招惹"警察"，它真的在绕圈。然后——

[3360s - 3480s]

We saw a navy fucking destroyer. This is true. Swear to my kids. Ah, the U.S. military. Holy shit! I don't care what you say. There is a feeling of man, ain't nobody fucking with us. I mean, literally, Lex. This thing is now. Then we hear, follow the Dallas because the Dallas came out. That's slow, but they came and it followed the Dallas in, right? So we go in. We get to the dock. There's all these special navy investigate all. They separate us all. It's four of us. Separate us all. Put us in rooms. Not good stuff. Interviewing every one of us. What did you see? What color clothes? What move did they make? What happened? What was on the deck? Because intelligence was it is today. This is 1980. So holy shit! We're just trying to save people. Yeah. And the military just because the navy has another story about that. I was on the Coast Guard Cutter Jarvis, and I heard Fidel Castro open the missile silo doors. I read the teletype that came in because I was in the radio room, and again, because of my rank, and I had all the people on the ship that worked for me. They don't work for me, but I'd hang out with them, and they'd ask, "Hey, like, can you send a couple guys to do the painting and shit?" When that happened, we, the Coast Guard, go to DEFCON or they didn't call it DEFCON, but they call whatever it was. Our billet during a time of war was to join up with a certain aircraft carrier group, and our job, only on a Coast Guard, if this was the carrier, was to be right here and protect this quarter from ASW, anti-submarine warfare. We had good sonar on our ship, so our only job, because you know aircraft carriers go out with ten ships, they got one here, one here, one here, and here, and then they got two subs, and they got you know. So our job was right here.

【译】我们看到了一艘海军驱逐舰。这是真事，我可以对我的孩子发誓。啊，美军。我靠！不管你怎么说，那种感觉就是：没人敢惹我们。真的是这样，莱克斯。然后我们听到命令：跟着达拉斯号，因为达拉斯号开出来了。它慢，但开出来了，我们跟着达拉斯号进了港。我们到码头时，一堆海军特别调查人员把我们四个人全部分开，一人一间屋子。可不是好事。逐个审问我们每一个人：你看见什么了？什么颜色的衣服？他们做了什么动作？发生了什么？甲板上有什么？因为情报工作跟今天一样重要。这是1980年。我靠！我们只是想救人。是啊。军队嘛……说到海军，我还有另一个故事。我在海岸警卫队"贾维斯号"上服役时，听到过菲德尔·卡斯特罗（Fidel Castro）打开导弹发射井井盖。我读了传进来的电传电文，因为我在电讯室，也因为我的军衔——船上那些替我干活的人，其实不归我管，但我跟他们混得熟，他们会问："嘿，能派几个人来干点刷漆之类的活吗？"那件事发生时，我们海岸警卫队进入了战备等级——他们不叫 DEFCON，叫别的什么名目。战争时期我们的战位（billet）是编入某个航母战斗群，我们的任务——海岸警卫队唯一的任务——如果这是航母，就是待在这个位置，保护这一扇面免遭反潜战（ASW，anti-submarine warfare）威胁。我们舰上的声呐很好，所以我们唯一的任务就是这个。你知道航母出海带着十艘舰，这里一艘、那里一艘，还有两艘潜艇，等等。我们的任务就在这个位置。

[3480s - 3600s]

During time of war, so we were told head to your your billet, whatever it was, and all of a sudden you hear ooh the the frigging the the turbine. We had a fast boat. All of a sudden you hear those turbines wind up because we had two big airplane engines and turbines. When you heard those things go up, I used of course because I was running it. Okay, we're going on search and rescue. That was my first thing, and then they usually call me and say, "Get ready for this. This is coming up or whatever." So I didn't get that. We just that's when I went to the radio room and found out what was going on. And the teletype, Ronald Reagan said, "If you don't close those those those silo doors in five minutes, I'm going to bomb your country." You know, I got a fucking thing on my wall. I marched for Ronald Reagan in his inaugural parade. That's pretty wild. Yeah, it's wild. I was a brainwashed young kid too. You know, I was all gung ho on every single. You know, come on. But by the way, that's a part of being young. Yeah, is believing something strongly. I think you're right, and that's why young people are often the engines of revolutions, and I think that's good. Makes sense. When you're older and wiser, you see the complexity and the nuance of things. So you're you're thinking like young people, they don't get it. It's more complicated. It's so true. But then, like, it's speaking to the intelligent thing is like when you're wise and you see the nuance and complexity, you don't get, you don't do anything. I was just going to say that you get complacent. You need to be focused, passionate, a little bit dumb, and really believe in the thing, and get together to really create a revolution and make progress. So you need both in the world. You know, I follow what's going on around the world. Not that it, I don't even know. I mean, I I hate to see people die in these wars with like Iran and and of course Ukraine. And I have friends that I have a doctor, very prominent doctor who's a Ukrainian, and he goes.

【译】战争时期，于是我们奉命前往各自的战位。突然你听到涡轮——我们有艘快船，装着两台大飞机发动机和涡轮。那些东西轰起来的时候——当然是我管的。好，我们去执行搜救。那是我脑子里第一个念头，然后他们通常会打电话来说"准备一下，有任务要来了"之类。这次没打。我们就……那时我去了电讯室，才知道出了什么事。电传电文上写着，罗纳德·里根（Ronald Reagan）说："如果五分钟内不关上那些发射井井盖，我就轰炸你的国家。"你知道吗，我墙上还挂着个东西——我在罗纳德·里根的就职典礼游行里走过队列。挺狂的。是挺狂。我那时也是个被洗脑的年轻人，对每一件事都热血上头。不过话说回来，年轻就是这样，其中一部分就是强烈地相信某件事。我觉得你说得对，这正是为什么年轻人常常是革命的引擎，我认为这是好事。有道理。当你年纪大了、更有智慧，你会看到事物的复杂性和细微差别，于是你会想：年轻人不懂，事情更复杂。太对了。但反过来，说到聪明这件事——当你有了智慧、看到了复杂与细微，你反倒什么都不做了。我正想这么说：你会变得自满。你需要专注、热情、有点"傻"、真正相信那件事，并且聚在一起，才能真正掀起一场革命、取得进步。所以这个世界两种人都需要。你知道，我关注着世界局势。不是说我……我讨厌看到人们在战争中死去，比如伊朗，当然还有乌克兰。我有个朋友，一位很有声望的医生，他是乌克兰人，然后他说——

[3600s - 3720s]

There, he sends money. I mean, a lot of money. He sends supplies. He's he's been with me. I know him. I just saw him for Halloween. He's such a great Simon Vinarski, and he's such a great guy. He really is. What him the boat doctor? Him and his wife are doctors, and just really good people. You know, down to talk. You want to talk? You know, when you talked about what you do, music. I heard you should see Simon. Simon comes to where the card game, and one of doctor friends of mine has a piano. This guy, some guy, this guy named Alan. He sits down. He goes, "Oh Lord, it needs to be tuned." And so Simon sits down. None of us knew this. Holy concert level shit! He goes, "Piano is good." We fuck with that guy to this day. Yeah, you know, I unreal. You know, you got. I don't know what it is with you. There's so much. You grow up that way. No, you're like. I mean, I see. He would sit down. I mean, like you. I I think the Soviet upbringing is such that you get a package of things you're supposed to be good at: math. I mean, usually it's going to be like classical classical music with piano or violin or something like that. There's some basic sports like wrestling and. Oh really? Is that is that how it goes? I mean, I'm partially joking, but there is no. I don't think you are. He is chess. You know. Yeah, I watched the chess, the best chess player I've ever seen in my life, and I watched. I used to play chess. Pretty. You watched prisoners play chess. Wow, some really good chess players. This one guy was a murderer. He was from Russia. He killed his whole family, and he killed the neighbors. Like it was like psycho stuff. But good chess. Good. Listen to this. We we we got together, and he we were in the county jail. We got three chess or four chessboards or whatever. Put them out. Four guys who thought they were good put down. He was.

【译】——他就捐钱，我是说很多钱，还送物资。他跟我一起待过，我认识他，万圣节前刚见过他。Simon Vinarski，他真是个特别好的人，真的是。什么，那位"船医"？他和他的妻子都是医生，真是好人，随和，聊得来。你不是谈到你做的事吗，音乐。我听说了——你真该看看 Simon。Simon 来到我们打牌的地方，我一个医生朋友家里有架钢琴。有个家伙，一个叫 Alan 的，坐下来弹，说："老天，这琴该调音了。"于是 Simon 坐下来。我们没人知道他会弹。我靠，音乐会级别的水平！他说："钢琴没问题。"我们到今天还拿那家伙开玩笑。是啊，真不可思议。我不知道你们这类人是怎么回事，会的东西太多了。你是那样长大的？不，你就像……我是说，我看他坐下来那架势。我觉得苏联式的成长环境给你一揽子你"应该擅长"的东西：数学，通常还有古典音乐，钢琴或小提琴之类，再加上些基础体育，比如摔跤。真是那样吗？我一半是开玩笑，但也不全是。我不觉得你是在开玩笑。他嘛，是下棋。对。我看过人下棋，这辈子见过的最好的棋手，我看他下过——我以前也下棋。挺不错的。你在监狱里看囚犯下棋。是啊，有些真的很棒的棋手。有一个人是杀人犯，俄国人，杀了自己全家，还杀了邻居，心理变态那种事。但棋下得好。听着这个。我们凑到一起——那时我们在县监狱，摆出三副还是四副棋盘。四个自认为下得好的坐上去。他——

[3720s - 3840s]

Was not even looking at the boards, and he beat everyone in so like ten moves, thirteen moves. It was like it was, it was, it was funny. It was like he was watching TV. I didn't know what he did at that point. We knew he did something because we were all in the felony pod. That's called the bad pod. Like you know, you had to do a serious crime to be in there. And yes, you know, a couple murders, drug deals, whatever it is. And this guy was like, "I, I never see someone play chess like that. I didn't think he looked at the boards. He literally watching TV. Turn around. Yeah, go back. I don't know how to do that. Yeah, when you get to a certain level, like world class level, you could you have the board in your head. You don't. You can only just allocate like one percent of your brain to it, and you can. It's crazy. It's crazy. We taught you to play it in the hole and stuff like that, but it's that is just like to me, and I like stuff like that. I love to see the brain get pushed to its limit, and I really believe the brain can do so. Listen, the strongest I've ever had to be in my brain was when I did not kill myself, and that almost broke me. And again, it was not being in there. It was not knowing if I'll ever get out. That was the torture. The torture wasn't being there because if I had a date, you put a date in your head, and you can figure whatever the date. It could be a year. It could be five years. When you don't have a date of getting out of that hole, you think you're going to die, and you watch. I watch people go crazy, Lex. I watched one friend of mine on the yard. Nice guy. Guys to work out together. Take feces and draw feces.

【译】——眼睛根本不看棋盘，十步、十三步就把所有人将死。那场面很搞笑，他就像在看电视剧。我当时不知道他犯过什么事，只知道他犯过事，因为我们都在"重罪监区"（felony pod），就是所谓坏蛋监区——你得犯下重罪才能进那儿，几桩谋杀、毒品交易什么的。这家伙就是这样。我从来没见过有人那样下棋，我觉得他都没看棋盘，真的像在看电视剧。转过身去又下一盘。人怎么能做到这样？对，到一定水平，世界级的水平，棋盘就在你脑子里，你只需分出大概百分之一的脑力给棋盘就行，然后就……太不可思议了。我们在"洞"里也教人下这一类东西。对我来说就是——我喜欢这种东西，我爱看大脑被推到极限，我真相信大脑做得到。听着，我的脑子经受过的最强考验，是我没有自杀的那一次，那几乎把我击垮。再说一遍：不是被关在里面，而是不知道还能不能出去——那才是酷刑。酷刑不在于待在那儿，因为如果有个日期，你在脑子里定下日期就能盘算，一年也好，五年也好。当你没有走出那个"洞"的日期，你会觉得自己会死在里面，而且你眼看着——我看着人发疯，莱克斯。我看着我的一个朋友在放风场上——很好的人，一起健身的伙计——拿粪便，用粪便画……

[3840s - 3960s]

The whole unit was like, you know, like they have in in the hole they have tiers, like, and they lock the front. You can never leave yourself without being handcuffed behind the back, and certain things with people you have to have two guards and all this. This guy was right, drawing demonic pictures on his walls with feces, and I knew this guy pretty well. Normal guy, man, nice. He cracked or snapped and whatever, and boy, I'll never forget that to this day because it makes me think, why didn't I really lose it, or did I? Maybe I used to say, maybe I am fucking nuts, but I don't know. It's like those are the things, but that I think it also teaches me is what I can handle. That was your chess match, and you, you, you, you won that one, Lex. I don't. The life I'm talking about is so crazy. I live, yeah, and you've lived so many lives. I mean, you messed with the coast guard. It's like you've you've been through so many journeys. It's incredible, and it's funny because from the coast guard to prison to to being the robberies and jewelry and life has treated me so crazy, and I'm alive. I've been shot. I've been stabbed twice. I have open heart surgery, eleven hours back surgery, two other back surgeries, a neck surgery. Look at this. So I'm here, and I pull up my shirt from the top of my back to here to the bottom of my tail. Is a big scar because they opened me up because I hurt myself in the service. I crushed my spine. I fell thirty feet into a fish hole. If it wasn't fish, I don't know what would happen. You know, I mean, still, you know, packed fish. I don't remember anything for three weeks. The coast guard captain, my the guy that I liked, saved my life because he once it happened. We're on this other ship. He turned the coast guard ship.

【译】整个监区……你知道，"洞"里是分层（tiers）的，前面锁死，你永远不能自己出监室，必须反铐双手，对待某些人还得两个狱警押着，诸如此类。这家伙就是在墙上用粪便画恶魔图案，而我跟这个人挺熟的。正常人，老兄，很好的人。他崩溃了、垮掉了。我到今天都忘不了那一幕，因为这让我想：为什么我没有真的失控？还是其实失控了？也许我该说，也许我他妈就是疯了，谁知道呢。就是这类事情，但我想它也教会了我：我能承受什么。那就是你的棋局，莱克斯，而那一局，你赢了。我说的这种生活太疯狂了。是啊，你活过了好几辈子：我是说，你待过海岸警卫队……就好像你经历了那么多段人生旅程，不可思议。而有趣的是，从海岸警卫队到监狱、再到抢劫珠宝，命运对我离奇至极，而我还活着。我中过枪，被捅过两次，做过开胸手术、十一小时的后背手术、另外两次背部手术、一次颈部手术。看这个。所以我还在这儿，我掀起衣服，从背上沿一直到尾椎底，是一条大疤，因为他们把我整个打开了，因为我在服役时受的伤。我压碎了脊椎，从三十英尺高摔进鱼舱。要不是舱里有鱼，我不知道会怎样。是啊，就是说，那儿装满了鱼。我三个星期什么都不记得。海岸警卫队的舰长——我喜欢的那位——救了我的命。事情一发生——我们在另一艘船上——他把海岸警卫队的船——

[3960s - 4080s]

Around and raced away from us. Sent the helicopter up to get me. And the reason he did that is because he couldn't have made it. He wanted to get the helicopter. Couldn't take me straight to the to the land. It had a fuel again. So he got as far as he could on the with the ship to land the chopper, let it fuel, and then take me to the hospital. And and then I was medevac from Alaska to California, C-130 from there, and then I ended up in Bethesda Naval Hospital in Washington. And they were gonna operate back then, but thank God they did. And I was in such great shape. I mean, young, strong, you know. And I'm so glad I waited because the technology back then, compared to when I got my surgery in 2018, and then my open heart was in 2019, triple bypass, and I'm so lucky because I feel great. And I still do everything I want. People say you still party. Yep, so fucking lutely. You you you you smoke cigars. You damn skippy! I smoke cigars. Do you have any regrets looking back? No, and I'll tell you why. Regret is a emotion that has no justification to be, just like hate. I don't hate anybody because hate only hurts you. Regret is like that. Now, regret defines if you regret your past or anything you've done, then you got to regret. You got to not like your future. Because you've what you did is making your future. Listen, I couldn't be who I am unless everything happened in the exact way it did. And you know everything we do. I mean, I I you know I tell even guys who work with me and and I say, I'm listen. If you're gonna make mistakes, I don't care. Just don't keep making the same mistake over.

【译】——调过头，加速驶离我们，派直升机起飞来接我。他这么做是因为船来不及赶到：他要的是让直升机到位，又不能直接把我送上岸——油又不够了。所以他驾船能送多远送多远，让直升机落地、加满油，再送我去医院。然后我从阿拉斯加被医疗后送到加州，从那里上 C-130 运输机，最后落到华盛顿的贝塞斯达海军医院（Bethesda Naval Hospital）。他们当时就打算做手术，感谢上帝没做成。我那时状态特别好：年轻、强壮。我很庆幸自己等了，因为当年的技术，没法跟我2018年做手术、2019年开胸做三重搭桥时的技术比。我太幸运了，因为现在感觉很好，想做的事全都还能做。有人问：你还去浪？对，当然他妈的去。你还抽雪茄？那还用说（damn skippy）！我抽雪茄。你回头看，有没有什么遗憾？没有，我告诉你为什么。遗憾（regret）是一种没有存在理由的情绪，就像恨一样。我不恨任何人，因为恨只会伤害你自己。遗憾也一样。遗憾意味着：如果你为你的过去、为你做过的任何事感到遗憾，那你就得一直遗憾下去，你就不会喜欢你的未来，因为你做过的一切正在塑造你的未来。听着，如果不是每一件事都精确地以它发生的方式发生，我就不会是今天的我。我们做的每一件事……我连跟我一起干活的人都说：听着，你要犯错，我不在乎，只是别一遍又一遍地犯同一个错——

[4080s - 4200s]

Over and over, because then you're an idiot. But if you keep try, if you don't make mistakes, you're not trying. Number one, period. Because nobody's perfect, so try it. I've messed up so much in my life, and if if you ever notice, the things we don't or we think about are not about money. At least me. Oh, I I missed this deal and give a. It's gonna come again. Something will come again, and I always looked like that, so it was never about the money. It was always about somebody I loved or somebody I cared about a lot that I hurt. I felt I hurt, and I always learned I didn't hurt him as much as I think I did. Cause they just look at me and they go, "What?" And they hug me. "Dad, I love you, man." You know, there's uh, there's good in all of us. There, there really is that. You know, there's a couple people I don't think there is, and I always say that I see people that I'm telling you, Lex. They they don't belong in the street. They would look for the. Listen, I that's why I'm I'm a big believer in rehabilitation. 【译】——他们把情报给了我：钱在哪儿；店里不会有人，只有一个店员，不会有别人进店。当然，这些门道是我后来多次抢劫才自己彻底摸透的。于是我去看那家店，稍微踩了点（cased），不像我后来手艺精熟时踩得那么细。果然，在特定时间没人来没人往，因为你必须真正吃透"踩点"这件事。店面是最关键的：得知道会不会有人来，附近有什么警察在活动，有没有商场保安，会不会哪个时间有邮差来、UPS 快递来，无论什么——没有人。我进了店，好，我要动手了。而且我知道东西在哪儿——那些贵重的东西，正因为他想要保险金，他才真正希望被抢走的就是那些。而这其实是个大局——珠宝行业本身就是个大游戏，这个我后面再细讲。于是我走进店里，掏出枪。我没戴面具，但我随时改变自己的长相：那时我平时不戴眼镜，我就戴上眼镜；我留山羊胡或者不留，总让自己看上去有一点点不一样。好笑的是，人像辨认错得离谱：他们把我描述成六英尺二、红头发。你知道我那时185磅（约84公斤），是个状态正好的拳手。他们说我240磅？全是乱写的数字。我走进店里，柜台后面有个姑娘。我事先知道会是个姑娘，但她不知道要发生什么。我说："这是抢劫。"她往后一缩，居然伸手去够枪。我动作很快，立刻翻过柜台。我看着她：你疯了吗？你疯了吗？我手里有枪。我把她按在身前，我带了束带（zip ties），把她捆了起来。就这样。东西都在哪儿、后门在哪儿、店里格局如何，我全知道。我不需要像后来的抢劫那样进店前做那么多功课。车在后面等着。我知道什么时候绝对没人：没有垃圾清运，也没有别的什么。果然，我就——

[4440s - 4560s]

Just take, put everything in a pillowcase, put it in there. Left probably a minute, minute and a half. And I ended up keeping one hundred and fifty thousand in my pocket. And this is in nineteen, maybe eighty nine, and eighty eight or eighty nine. And I go, whoa, this is this is nice. 【译】——把所有东西装进一个枕套里，装好就走。前后大概一分钟、一分半。另外，我还凭一种聪明劲儿掌握了太阳的方位——太阳东升西落，当太阳处在某个精确角度时，你从外面看不进玻璃橱窗。这在抢劫里非常重要。这个我懂。我拿着东西回到纽约，把珠宝交给了另一个黑帮关系人，我们把账分了。最后我自己落袋了十五万美元。那是 nineteen……大概是89年，88或89年。我说：哇，这买卖不错。我是说，那可是上头安排好的局。所以我说：我要再来一次。这是钱啊，而且是老板们发的话。我当时是甘比诺犯罪家族（Gambino crime family）的关联者，我的老板们告诉我：你在纽约任何地方都别动手——全都有保护，很多店都被保护着。明白。所以我从来没动过在纽约作案的念头。事实上，今天一听到有人在纽约抢劫，我就说：他们有麻烦了。到今天也是，因为那些店在交保护费。第47街（Forty Seventh Street）的大部分铺面都是。很多人以为47街就是钻石交易所，"哦，你要买钻石就去那儿"。不，那是 Donald Trump 去挨宰的地方，那不是你真正该买钻石的地方。你真正该买钻石的对象，是那些真正去戴比尔斯（De Beers）拿货的人——有些买家是直接去矿上的。说到戴比尔斯这家公司——戴比尔斯是世界最大的钻石公司。你知道俄国当时真要放开新矿，他们发现了钻石矿。我不知道背后达成了什么交易，但戴比尔斯现在把它们收入囊中。他们之所以出手，是因为俄方威胁要砸盘。那么——什么珠宝最值钱？你提到了钻石。你当时找的是什么？优先要的是黄金，还是钻石，还是什么？

[4560s - 4680s]

Were the most valuable. You mentioned diamonds. What what kind of stuff were you looking for? Prioritizing was the gold, is it diamonds, or what? This is like in the back somewhere, or what? Yeah. Wow, where they hide their diamonds is amazing. I've seen I've seen them hide them in false drawers, in desks, in false floorboards, and I get them, but because I know what I'm doing. But they all have a box like that's where they sell, and most the the jewelers I would hit would be wholesalers to other jewelers, and did have a retail location of something like that, and which is smart, is where they usually do their business. My biggest mistake in the whole jewelry industry is not opening a store in California on Rodeo Drive myself, and using some of the stuff there without non-signature pieces. But the most valuable, obviously, for a person of my of what I did was diamonds, and not even the exorbitant diamonds not three, four carats. They use usually about a two carat, carat and a half, very you know VVS one or with with a D color, I mean the best you can get. I've seen diamonds go two carat diamond go for you know ten grand. I seen a two carat diamond go for a hundred and fifty thousand. That's just going to show you the difference in cut, quality, clarity that goes along. I went to the GIA Institute. What's that? GIA Institute is a school for diamond gemological institute. It's the it's a school for diamonds. Well, listen to this, even better. I went to the school under the table. It was I paid ten thousand dollars. This is the craziest part. There were three other guys in there, and we were all criminals. I mean, you had to be a criminal. You're paying on.

【译】好，就钻石来说，批发商的珠宝店里永远会有一盒钻石，看上去像个旧卡片盒，就像棒球卡那种长盒子，里面装着信封，都是散装裸钻。所以这些不摆在店面？是藏在后面什么地方，对吧？对。哇，他们藏钻石的地方真是五花八门：我见过藏在书桌的暗抽屉里、藏在地板的暗格夹层里，我都能拿到，因为我懂行。但他们人人都有那么一个盒子，那是他们卖货的存货。我下手的大多数珠宝商都是面向其他珠宝商的批发商，同时也带着一处零售门面之类的——这很聪明，那通常是他们做买卖的地方。我在整个珠宝行业里最大的失误，就是没自己在加州罗迪欧大道（Rodeo Drive）开一家店，把一些不带签名标识的货用在那里。但对我干的这一行来说，最值钱的显然是钻石，而且甚至不是那种天价钻，不是三四克拉的。通常就是两克拉、一克拉半左右，VVS1 净度或者再配个 D 色，就是你能拿到的最好的东西。我见过一颗两克拉的钻石卖一万美元，也见过一颗两克拉的卖十五万美元。这恰恰说明切工、品质、净度带来的差别。我上过 GIA 学院。那是什么？GIA 学院，宝石学院（gemological institute），是教钻石的学校。听着，还有更绝的：我是走后门进去上的，付了一万美元。最疯狂的是：班上还有另外三个家伙，我们全是罪犯。我是说，必须是罪犯——你可是得在桌子底下花钱才进得去——

[4680s - 4800s]

The table to go to a GIA's to Gemological Institute is actually where they accredit jewelers, and they'll they'll be a GIA's, and and you learn all the intricacies of the diamond. You watch them cut diamonds are beautiful, and and I I try to emphasize diamonds. You know now they have laser cut diamonds, and they also have factory made diamonds now, and they're beautiful, and in fact you should buy them. Anybody with the naked eye who's not into the you know maybe some billionaire somewhere, what why wouldn't you buy them? You can't tell. First of all, they have what they call Lazare Kaplan diamonds, and they broke off. Lazare Kaplan diamonds are laser cut diamonds and have serial numbers in them, but you can't see the serial on them unless you have a ten power microscope. So obviously, when I knew there were laser cut diamonds, I don't want to rob that place. You know, I mean, that's all serial number diamond. Now people say, "Oh, but the diamond, my diamond has a birth certificate." Take your birth certificate and shove it up your ass because it means absolutely nothing to me because I could go to another GIA guy and they'll put it off a hair or two and certified that as the birth certificate of that diamond and now it's a new diamond because it's subjective to a degree. It's not an exact science, like you know. So, but diamonds, take a piece of glass. You ever see a really window break perfect and it's like that inside? It's so perfect. Diamond cutters are like that, and when I say unbelievable, I mean to see them work to take the smallest of small, it it just blows you away that they could be that talented. Like who brings someone in to screw that up? You know how much money are you screwing up? Yeah, you know it's not it's something that's amazing to me, but looking at the diamond industry and seeing again, I don't know how much is man made, you know, hype marketing diamonds. Because you know, back in all the pressure.

【译】——桌子底下花钱去上 GIA，宝石学院（Gemological Institute），其实就是给珠宝商做认证的地方，会有 GIA 证书，你在那儿学到钻石的一切门道。你看他们切钻石，太美了。我一直想强调钻石……现在有激光切工的钻石，还有工厂造的人造钻，都很漂亮，事实上你就该买。任何用肉眼看的人——除了哪个拿着专业设备的亿万富翁——凭什么不买？你根本分不出来。首先，有所谓拉扎尔·卡普兰（Lazare Kaplan）钻石，他们独立出去了。拉扎尔·卡普兰钻石是激光切的，里面刻有序列号，但除非用十倍放大镜，你看不见那些编号。所以很显然，我一知道哪家是激光刻号钻，我就不想抢那家——那全是带序列号的钻石。现在有人说："哦，可我的钻石有出生证（birth certificate）。"把你那出生证拿去塞屁股吧，因为对我来说它毫无意义：我可以去找另一个 GIA 的人，把石头再磨掉一丝，给它认证一张新的出生证——现在它就是一颗新钻石了，因为这行在一定程度上是主观的，它不是精确科学。钻石嘛——拿块玻璃，你见过窗户碎得特别齐整、断面就长那样吗？那么完美。钻石切割师就是那样。我说不可思议，是看他们干活、处理那些最小中的最小的原石，那种才华能把你惊呆。谁会请人进去把这种活儿搞砸？你知道那会砸掉多少钱？是啊，这对我来说就是奇迹。但看看钻石行业，再想想——我不知道这里面有多少是人造的营销泡沫，炒作出来的钻石。因为你知道，回到那种……压力。

[4800s - 4920s]

Stones that were used for something back in certain times, especially like copper and even silver and stuff, and if a conductive and you know that. Now, I mean, it's so there's so much done with man-made. At what point is is it all bullshit? Money is, you know, where does money get value? Period. It's all human illusion, right? But we all agree that has value, and the diamonds are the same. We all somehow agree they have value. In prison, they have a commodity: stamps. Now, it used to be cigarettes, and I was in when that happened too. And then they took all cigarettes off the yards in prison. In fact, in the whole prison system, there were more stabbings over when they took cigarettes off than drugs, and really went after drugs. It's because it's obviously one of the most addicting chemicals. Oh, I thought you meant because you mess with the monetary system. No, no, but they they had it as a monetary. Now they have what they call stamps. Literally, stamps, postage stamps. So when I was in a book of postage stamps would be six dollars, but you'd break them up, and no matter what, that book of stamps was five dollars. So twenty stamps, every one of them was a quarter, and you'd literally break them up into a little square, and that is a book of stamps. I, of course, being a creative person, I became a bookie because I was a bookie on the streets in prison. I would have thousands of books of stamps, and I would run a bookmaking operation. Literally, the football tickets where I started my whole kind of career. Actually, I used to sell books of stamps. I would sell twenty five books for a hundred bucks. 【译】——石头，在过去的某些时代本来是有实际用途的，尤其是铜啊、甚至银啊这类导电的东西，这你知道。现在，人造的东西这么多，到哪一步就全是扯淡了？钱也一样，你知道，钱的价值从哪儿来？说到底，全是人类的共同幻觉，对吧？但我们全都同意它有价值，钻石也一样——我们不知怎么就都同意它们有价值。在监狱里，有一种硬通货：邮票。以前是香烟，我蹲监狱时正好赶上那次变革。后来他们把香烟从监狱的放风场全面撤掉了。事实上，在整个监狱系统里，因为取消香烟引发的捅人事件比毒品还多，而扫毒他们可是真下了力气的。因为香烟显然是最成瘾的东西之一。哦，我还以为你是说你搅乱了货币体系。不不，但他们确实把它当货币用。现在他们用所谓"邮票"——就是字面意义上的邮票，邮资邮票。我那会儿，一册邮票定价六美元，但拆开来算，无论如何一册按五美元算。二十枚一册，每枚算两毛五，你真的可以一枚一枚拆开来当零钱用，这就是"一册邮票"。我嘛，天生有创造力，当上了庄家（bookie），因为我在街上就干庄家，在监狱里也是。我手里有几千册邮票，经营着一摊收注生意。真的，足球彩票——我整个"事业"某种程度上就是从那儿起步的。其实我还卖邮票册：一百块卖你二十五册。你为什么不找我买？你白得五册，因为我量大。于是我每到一座监狱，都有人上门。我打听清楚谁手里有邮票，然后说："听着，一百块你给我多少册？"因为这就叫街钱（street money）。然后我打电话叫人"给他汇两百美元"，我就买下五十册邮票。什么叫"街对街"（street to street）？

【译】监狱里不能持有现金。但比方说你是放风场上的庄家，或者手里有邮票的人——我说：嘿，莱克斯，你好啊，我是拉里。我让我的人给你的人两百块，你把邮票给我。你说：行，街对街（street to street）。我就会让我在外面的人——我说：汇款。你说："我在做法律工作，麻烦给这位——"嗯，我不会说"莱克斯"——"给这个人汇两百美元。"我的人立刻就办。他们一办妥，钱清了，你就给我五十册邮票。这下我有了本钱，就可以开所谓"商店"了。监狱里真有人开商店，那是一整个社会，就像你说的，货币体系就是邮票。但价值守住了。有意思，邮票的价值居然守住了。好笑的是，随着邮票涨价，现在一册好像值十美元了，随行就市呗。所以有通胀。有通胀。说真的，当年出来这事的时候我可没这么想过。还有，听着，我把成千上万册邮票藏在门把手里面——把门拆下来，塞在墙壁后面的夹层里——因为狱警逮住你就完了：按规定你只能持有三册邮票。我有几千册，货真价实的几千册，值一万美元还是多少。那就是我的营生（hustle）。首先，它让你的脑子不停转；其次它就是货币。他们问：什么意思？我说：什么都能当钱使。你要方便面（soups）？有人想押三包面。方便面赔五比一：你押一包面，赢五包；押三包，赢十五包回来。这让所有人都开心。事实上，连监狱自己都不太认真查这个。你知道的，真想收拾你的时候，他们自然会收拾你，就是这么回事。这显然是一场猫鼠游戏，但他们不按规则玩。好，你讲了那次抢劫。后来是怎么升级的？你第一次真正抢珠宝是什么时候？好，那次之后——

【译】——那次抢劫。我回了纽约，我说："哇，这是15万，轻松钱。"该上交的我上交给了该上交的人。而人们不理解黑手党的一点是：必须得有我这种人——会思考的人、能挣钱的人、能想出赚钱路子的人。我们那时抢仓库，靠的是在里头安插欠了我赌债的人。他是个仓库经理，出了事，第一件事就是赌债还不上。我说："你最好去找个人想办法，因为我要我的钱。"他说："那我怎么办？"我说："我认识几个能给你放高利贷（loanshark）的人。"知道什么是高利贷（loansharking）吧？对。这么说吧：我最早的几间酒吧就是这么到手的。顺便说一句，如果我借你一万美元，你每周得给我三百块，每周五。每个周五，我要三百块。这钱可不算还本金。给你六个月——通常给我六个月，因为我要的就是六个月。这种他会来找你——很多酒吧老板之类的人都会来找你，因为在多数州，酒类管理局一天之内就能把你关停。你不交税——吧台上流过的每一块钱你以为都进了口袋，在佛州，少交那六分钱的税，他们就会上门封你的门，你就完了，真的完了。所以他来问："我怎么办？我急需两万，马上。"什么时候要？昨天——他们今晚要封门，我今晚就必须开业，这是大周末。成交。你知道规矩吧？三个点（三分利）。行。好，六百。我每周五派个人到你那儿，我要六百块。别让他在你那儿等——让他直接来见你。六百块，一分不能少。于是那家伙每周五都把钱送来。我懂这门生意：他是在为那扇门付钱。他纯粹是个糟糕的生意人：不交税，钱都花在应召女郎还是什么鬼名堂上。果然，每周六百、六百、六百。

【译】——真的，我因为自慰被扔进了"洞"里。听这节目的人多半会说："你什么意思？"对，我一个人待在监室里。点名出了错，他们把我们锁进去过夜。我手里有一本色情杂志。你怎么办？我是说，这算什么事嘛？讲道理。于是我自慰完了，完事儿。凌晨一点，"哐"——"Lawton，回监室（lock up），回监室！"叫你 lock up 是因为——监狱进入一级戒备（lockdown）时，你不戴上手铐就不能出那间监室。回监室。说起来，我的好友 Paulie Tallini，到今天还上我的节目，他可能是我认识的最好的律师。论做刑事法律这一行——他执业三十年，其中二十七年是因为毒品案进去的。又是非暴力犯罪，我觉得这太疯了，这个我后面再讲。于是 Paulie 看到新闻就说："拉里这回又干了什么？"就这样，我被带去了"洞"里——监狱里叫 SHU（special housing unit，特别监禁区）。我下去，那个警察在笑，SHU 的中尉进来看见我也开始笑。你笑个屁啊，老兄？我蹲在"洞"里，在一个匣子里，被扒光了关押。他们刚把我塞进洞里。"对啊，Lawton，你因为打飞机被抓了。"什么？什么意思？确实有那种对着女狱警干的、站在门口耍流氓的变态。可我一个人躺在床上，他们要我怎么办？你知道吗，说起来，美国的监狱系统——哦，你这是勾起我的话头了——它是如此——

【译】——糟糕，以至于大多数国家甚至不会因为越狱未遂起诉你。因为想逃是天性。接受一切、然后说"好吧，我认了，我这辈子完了，找个相好、混过日子、去监狱工厂（转写误作 unicorn，应为 UNICOR）"——那才不正常。你要明白这一点。所以后来就演变成：在我那个年代，私藏手机就算"越狱未遂"，他们会给你加一项外面的刑事指控，想借此判你五年。现在这项降格了，因为监狱里手机实在太多了。话说回来，我在"洞"里。"你因为打飞机被抓了。"对啊，怎么着？我打了。你可能早就看出来了，我不是躺平认栽的人。我是斗士，我喜欢斗。身体上也斗——不斗了，我老了，我62岁了。我说："你开玩笑呢？我起诉了司法部长阿尔贝托·冈萨雷斯（Alberto Gonzales），起诉了联邦监狱局局长哈利·拉平（Harley Lappin）。我记得他们的名字。我起诉了我所在大区的区域局长，起诉了我那座监狱的典狱长，起诉了那些狱警。我起诉了七个人。"他们怎么办？想办法把案子驳回。我知道怎么绕过去——法院。果然，没驳成。他们把我关在"洞"里、关在 SHU 里。他们管它叫"特别"监区——这地方到底特别在哪儿？就这样，我在洞里，我告他们。他们第一招是给我转监，然后"弄丢"我的法律文书。行，我有办法绕过。我接着斗，接着斗。最后他们来找我："听着，把这个案子撤了，我们会改政策，某些做法我们会改。"而我要的就是这个，我只想——真的，你上网一搜就有，谷歌一下就能看到，因为我起诉过。我那时不怕。后来发生的事——
