Again, I was not right what I did to her either. Let's. But don't tell me to rat on a person. I often tell people there's only one thing I have, and they took like I said six million bucks. They offered me to keep my money. And what do you mean? Can you tell the story of? Yeah, when I got arrested by the feds, I got arrested by the FBI, of course. Helicopter over the house, the whole works, you know. Then it came out in the newspapers. Organized crime involved in the biggest jewel ring in the United States. That's where the FBI said I was the best jewel robber ever did it. The when I got caught, they wanted me to rat, and they said you could keep your money, and you'll do about two years. So you chose not to snitch. It's just something my dad, my dad, my parents gave me. You, the only thing you're gonna have is your word in this life, and it is the truth. How many people don't snitch in prison? I'd say there might be a good percentage, but fifty-fifty, maybe. You know, depending. So a lot of people would snitch. Oh yeah, you'll see a lot of snitches. You'll see, you know, the government wins most its cases. I'm talking feds now.

【译】那我当初真该多干点。还是那个女人——这是我出狱之后的事——FBI 进了那座监狱，从她办公室割走了一块沾着精液的地毯。她跟一个囚犯——我认识那个囚犯——鬼混、上床，在她任职期间就跟这个囚犯发生关系；等那人出狱之后，他们还在继续。那家伙也是个人渣，因为后来发生的事是——他又被捕了，为了给自己的案子脱罪，把她供了出来。就是这么回事。你明白我的意思吧？他说：「哦，我知道，我跟她睡过，她在里面跟人乱搞。」她呢？收拾东西就走，从此再没在监狱露面。再说一遍，我对她干的事也不对，这我们放一边。但别叫我出卖别人。我常跟人讲：人这一辈子只有一样东西是自己的——而他们拿走了，我说过，六百万美元。他们当时提出让我保住我的钱。（莱克斯：）这是什么意思？能讲讲这个故事吗？（拉里：）好。我被联邦政府逮捕的时候，当然是 FBI 抓的：直升机在我家上空盘旋，全套阵仗。然后报纸登出来了：有组织犯罪卷入美国史上最大珠宝盗窃团伙。就是在那儿，FBI 说我是有史以来最厉害的珠宝劫匪。我被抓后，他们要我当「老鼠」，说：你可以保住你的钱，而且只需服刑两年左右。（莱克斯：）所以你选择了不出卖别人。（拉里：）那是我爸爸、我父母给我的东西：你这辈子唯一能拥有的就是你的诺言（your word），这是真的。（莱克斯：）监狱里有多少人不出卖别人？（拉里：）我估计比例可能还不错，但也就五五开吧，也许，看情况。（莱克斯：）所以很多人会告密。（拉里：）哦是的，你会见到很多告密者。你会看到——你知道的，政府赢下他们大部分案子。我现在说的是联邦层面。

[8400s - 8520s]

Especially, the government wins their cases. Here's why: they'll arrest twenty people. Eighteen take pleas or flip or give testimony against that guy, and and the other guy one might win the case, and the other loses it. But they got nineteen convictions out of twenty. That's why the government has such a high success rate in the trial. They're good. Don't get me wrong. When the federal government comes after you, you better run because that indictment says United States of America versus Lawrence Lawton. Think of that. Think of that. That's some heavy shit. Yeah, and they don't arrest you prematurely. See, what happens in states is that one, they don't have the funding to catch a guy like me. Second, they will arrest you when it happens, and now they're under the clock. If you know the law, they have you have speedy trial laws. So, somebody like me who knows the law is going to push the speedy trial. I'm not. Most people go to court. The first thing to do is, lawyer gets up. You're on. Yeah, we'd like to waive speedy. Yeah, yeah, everyone loves it. You think, oh, it's good, good. The lawyer sold you a bunch of shit saying, oh, the longer we go, the better. Not in a state case. A lot of times, if the states will arrest you prematurely because they want to, there's different things involved. Politics is a who's who's the state attorney needs more arrest, and then they don't have the right evidence, and you'll beat it at trial. If you don't waive speedy trial, when you waive speedy trial, it's only six months, 180 days. That goes quick when you know they have to have all their ducks in order, and you got a good lawyer. Now, good lawyer. A lot of lawyers do that for money because every hearing after that, everything you want is money, money, money. Trust me. And my lawyers were were good, and that's how I ended up beat. But I beat my own case. I ended up firing my lawyer, and I beat my own case because I knew what to call a.

【译】尤其是政府，他们就是能赢。原因在这儿：他们一次逮捕二十个人，十八个认罪交易（plea）、反水，或者出庭指证那个人；剩下两个，一个也许赢了官司，另一个输了。但他们二十个里拿到了十九个定罪。这就是政府庭审胜率那么高的原因。他们确实厉害，这我不否认。当联邦政府冲着你来的时候，你最好赶紧跑，因为那份起诉书上写的是「美利坚合众国 诉 Lawrence Lawton」。想想这个。想想这个。这可是够分量的东西。是啊。而且他们不会过早抓你。州里的情况则是：第一，他们没有追捕我这种人的经费；第二，他们一发生案子就抓你，从此就上了倒计时。如果你懂法律——有「快速审判」法（speedy trial laws）——像我这种懂法的人就会逼着快速审判。大多数人上法庭，第一件事就是律师站起来：「好的，我们放弃快速审判权（waive speedy）。」对对对，人人都这么干。你以为，哦，这样好、好。律师卖给你一堆屁话，说什么「拖得越久越好」。在州案子里不是这样的。很多时候，州里会过早抓人，因为各有各的算盘——政治因素，谁当政、州检察官需要更多逮捕数——然后他们证据不齐，你在庭审上就能赢。只要你别放弃快速审判权——一旦放弃，就只剩六个月、180 天。日子过得很快，尤其当你知道他们必须把一切准备到位、而你又有个好律师的时候。说到好律师——很多律师那么做是为了钱，因为之后的每一次听证、你要的每一样东西，都是钱、钱、钱。信我。我的律师们是好的，我最后也赢了。但我是自己赢的自己的案子：我最后把律师解雇了，自己把案子打赢了，因为我知道该怎么援引——

[8520s - 8640s]

924C is the the thing that would have killed me hard was the gun charge. It's called a 924C. When you use a gun in a commission of a felony, it's big time. And for for the first offense, you get you get five years. Every offense after that, you get twenty years running wild. So just for my gun, I would have got sixty five years. Because you're you're talking about the value of your word and not snitching. I have to go back to New York and the and the mob. Can you tell your connection to the mob? Sure, absolutely. I was with the Gambino crime family, and I got when I was growing up. I grew up in the Bronx, and in a Bronx neighborhood, you had a lot of different connected people. We I lived in in a working class in Locust Point in the Bronx. This is back before, actually. John Gotti killed Paul Castellano in eighty five, December of eighty five, and I was on medical leave because I fell in the Coast Guard on a Coast Guard ship out in the Bering Sea, and I hurt myself pretty bad. That's why I'm a retired veteran right now. I'm a disabled vet. When I got retired, they put me on back to Governors Island, New York, and I was staying there, but I didn't have to work, didn't do any. So I'm right back to where I was when I was a kid. I start working with the Gambinos through a connection. First thing I was doing, I was muscling a card game or bouncing for a card game underground card game in Queens and on Union Turnpike in Luke's Piano Lounge. And then I got better at got more and more. That became a bookie, and I was I learned from a guy named Mac. Mac was the biggest bookie in New York. He was what they call a layoff guy. That layoff guy would take action from all the other bookies. How the bookmaking business works is if Lex puts a hundred thousand dollars on this team, he don't want to take that because then he's a gambler. He needs to get somebody else to put a hundred thousand on the other team. A bookie makes a.

【译】——924C。真正能把我置于死地的是持枪罪，叫 924C 条款。在实施重罪的过程中使用枪支，那是重罪中的重罪。第一次违犯，判五年；之后每一次，叠加二十年、连续执行（running wild）。所以光是持枪罪，我就会吃六十五年。（莱克斯：）因为你在谈你的诺言的价值、谈不出卖别人，我得回到纽约、回到黑手党的话题。能讲讲你和黑手党的关系吗？（拉里：）当然，完全可以。我跟的是甘比诺犯罪家族（Gambino crime family），那是长大以后的事。我在布朗克斯长大，布朗克斯的街区里有很多不同的「圈内人」（connected people）。我住在布朗克斯洛克斯特角（Locust Point）的一个工薪社区。那其实是在——约翰·高蒂（John Gotti）八五年十二月干掉保罗·卡斯特拉诺（Paul Castellano）——之前的事。当时我正在休病假，因为我在海岸警卫队服役时、在白令海上的一艘海岸警卫队船上摔了下来，伤得很重。这就是为什么我现在是退役老兵，是伤残退伍军人。退役后，他们把我安置回纽约的总督岛（Governors Island）。我住在那儿，但不用工作，什么都不用干。于是我一头回到了小时候的状态。通过一层关系，我开始跟甘比诺家族干活。最开始干的活，是在皇后区联合干道（Union Turnpike）上 Luke's Piano Lounge 的地下牌局看场子、当保安。然后我越干越大，后来成了庄家（bookie）。我师从一个叫 Mac 的人。Mac 是纽约最大的庄家，是所谓的「分注庄家」（layoff guy）。分注庄家接所有其他庄家倒出来的赌注。收注这门生意的逻辑是：如果 Lex 在这支球队上押十万美元，庄家不想吃下这一注，因为那样他就成了赌徒；他需要找别人在另一支球队上押十万，把盘口配平。庄家赚的是——

[8640s - 8760s]

Ten percent. So a bookie's goal is to take, let's just say, a million dollars. Use a number. He wants five hundred thousand dollars on this team and five hundred thousand dollars on this team. He makes ten percent. He can care less who wins. What happens when a bookie gets seven hundred thousand dollars on this team and three hundred thousand on this team? If he don't lay it off to somebody who can handle that action, he becomes a gambler, and that's not what they want. So they'd come to Mac. They say when Mac died, the economy went down. He used to have Schubach out. I learned I was taking thousand dollar bets, and I was taking smaller bets, five hundred thousand. He's taking fifty thousand, hundred thousand dollars. So he was gambling. Well, he had so much money he didn't care. I mean, he was a smart. I mean, smartest guy I ever met. So, and he taught me the actual intricacies of the bookmaking business. This was you mentioned football. This is just betting on football. Oh, everything, everything, horses, football, the numbers, you name it, they bet on everything. Numbers, lottery, numbers, no, yeah. Well, we had the lottery before the lottery. In fact, to this day, the mob has bigger lottery than the lottery. Did you know that? Yeah, what's that mean exactly? Okay, the lottery. If you pick three numbers, see, lottery has different numbers. You can pick one number, a single action. You can pick two numbers. It's called a bleeder, or you can pick three numbers. Okay, you see it every night. Three numbers. Well, the mob does that, and the mob pays better than the state with no taxes and gives you credit. The mob pays six hundred to one if you hit three one three numbers. State gives you five hundred, and and you have to fill out a form. And they're legit, like honest with it. Who the mob? The mob, yeah. More so than the state. Yeah, there's a theme to this. We we made so much money that obviously you learn bigger and better. That's how I got the first gig with the robbing of the jewelry stores. That I was a trusted guy. I used to do heists. You know, used to rob things and to Ferguson warehouses, plumbing splices. I didn't tell you exactly how you do that.

【译】——百分之十。庄家的目标是，比如说，收一百万美元的注——用个整数——他想让这支队上押五十万、那支队上押五十万，他赚百分之十，谁赢他根本不在乎。那如果一个庄家在这支队收到七十万、那支队只有三十万，怎么办？如果不把失衡的注分注（lay off）给接得住的庄家，他就成了赌徒，而那不是他们想要的。所以大家都来找 Mac。他们说，Mac 死的时候，（那一带的）经济都跟着下滑了。他以前总把注分给 Schubach 出去。我那时候收一千美元的注，也收小注——五百、一千那种；他收的是五万、十万美元一注，所以他是在赌。可他钱多到不在乎。他是个聪明人——是我这辈子见过最聪明的人。是他教会了我收注这门行当真正的门道。（莱克斯：）你刚提到橄榄球——只赌橄榄球吗？（拉里：）哦，什么都赌，什么都赌：赛马、橄榄球、数字彩（numbers），你说得出的他们都押。数字彩、彩票、数字彩——对，是。我们在官方彩票出现之前就有「彩票」了。事实上到今天，黑手党的「彩票」比官方彩票盘子还大。你知道吗？（莱克斯：）嗯，这具体是什么意思？（拉里：）好，说官方彩票——彩票有不同玩法：可以选一个号，叫单选（single action）；可以选两个号，叫 bleeder；也可以选三个号。对，你每晚都看得到，三个数字。黑手党也开这个，而且赔率比州里高，不收税，还能赊账。选三个号全中，黑帮赔六百比一；州里只给五百，你还得填表。而且黑帮在这事上是讲信誉的，很规矩。（莱克斯：）谁讲信誉？黑手党？（拉里：）黑手党，对，比州政府还讲。（莱克斯：）嗯，这里有个共同的主题。（拉里：）我们赚了那么多钱，自然就学着干更大的、更好的。这就是我怎么拿到第一单抢珠宝店的活——因为我是个被信任的人。我以前干大买卖（heists），抢过 Ferguson 仓库（的货）、铅管接头（plumbing splices）之类的。具体怎么干的，我还没跟你说呢。

[8760s - 8880s]

So many little things we did to earn money. We were all hustlers. Again, I don't want anybody listening to your show to think I'm telling them it's right to do what we did. But justification, I always say, was these companies were screwing everybody. They were getting their insurance anyway. They still do it. It blows me away that right now we're taking a young person who has a drug addiction. He robs a bank. Now they give him a fine of ten thousand dollars. Well, that young man is never going to get out and pay that fine, and you're going to put him under a rock. And all you're doing is making that young man become a criminal again, because now he's going to work off the books. He's not going to pay his taxes. He's going to figure out ways to survive instead of that bank already wrote that money off. They got their government money for it. They already got them. Again, I'm not saying it's right to do it is, but there's fixes they can do that are pretty in your face or things that I notice that they can fix and to give a kid a shot again, because we're supposed to be the land of second chances. We're supposed to be a real rehabilitative country. We talked a bit about robbing jewelry stores, but can you give me some details of what it takes to do a successful? A lot of people think they can do it, and they can't, obviously, because you have to be a thinker more than anything. You know, when you start calculating the risk, the first thing you do at a robbery in jewelry stores, you don't look what's in there. You don't even look getting in there. You look, can I get away with it? My first thing is, can I exit this place? Is there is a spot to exit the place, and can I get away with it? Because if I can't get away at all, this means nothing. I don't care how much is in the store. Then you work backwards. You know, the first thing you do with a robbery is you work backwards. I can get away with it. Okay, now what's the next thing? Who's in there? How many people are in there? What are they going to do? And what's in there? You know, I always picked jewelry stores that were in.

【译】我们干过好多挣钱的零碎活儿，我们都是「跑活儿的人」（hustlers）。再说一遍，我不想让你节目的听众以为我在说我们干的那些事是对的。但说到给自己找的理由，我一直的说法是：那些公司本来就在坑所有人，保险他们反正照拿，现在还这样。让我震惊的是，如今我们对待一个有毒瘾的年轻人——他抢了银行，结果他们罚他一万美元。那个年轻人出狱后永远还不上那笔罚款，你就把他压到了石头底下。你所做的一切，只是逼着那个年轻人重新变成罪犯：从此他只能打黑工、不交税、想尽办法活下去——而银行早把那笔钱核销了，政府补偿的钱他们早拿到了。再说一遍，我不是说抢劫是对的，但明明有些明摆着的办法可以补救，有些我看得见的、他们可以改的地方，好给孩子再来一次的机会。因为我们本来应该是「第二次机会之国」，本该是一个真正讲改造的国家。（莱克斯：）我们聊过一些抢珠宝店的事，但你能讲讲要成功干一票需要什么吗？（拉里：）很多人以为自己干得了，其实干不了，原因很明显——最重要的是，你得是个会思考的人。当你开始计算风险的时候，抢珠宝店的第一件事，不是看店里面有什么，甚至不是看怎么进去。你要看的是：我能全身而退吗？我第一个要确认的是：我出得去这个店吗？有没有一个出口位置，能让我脱身？因为如果我根本跑不掉，其他一切都没有意义，店里有多少货我都不在乎。然后你倒着往回推。抢一票，你永远是倒着规划的：我能脱身——好，下一步是什么？里面都有谁？有多少人？他们会怎么做？里面有什么货？你知道，我总是挑那种开在——

[8880s - 9000s]

Plazas, or in a bigger place, because I could blend in. I could watch the store. If it's in a a grocery store, Publix Plaza, I could sit in that thing reading the paper. People think I'm waiting for somebody in the store, and I'm really watching the jewelry store. Because when you obviously it takes a lot, you have to really pay attention when you watch the jewelry store. There's, and the more you do, the better you got. I mean, I obviously you start planning. You do you when you're going to hit what direction? I was telling you, if a jewelry store is facing east, you're going to do that jewelry store in the morning because the sun rises in east, and then the sun hits the glass, and nobody can see in there. And you'll see a jewelry store who will who will put great displays right in front of the place to make it perfect for me to go in there. I love it. Jewelry stores would have a buzzer; they'll buzz you in. Great, I want the buzzer. They want me in there because they think they're going to sell me a nice ring, and nobody else can come in. Because I've had one time ten people come into the jewelry store. I had them all on the floor, and the reactions of the people you rob. First of all, none of the people I robbed ever came to court because they liked me. In fact, two older people said he was a nice guy when I robbed the jewelry store. Because a lot of them like you. Well, they I never would hurt. I never hurt civilians. I I don't believe in that. I don't believe it hurted innocents or elderly or women. I was telling you early, and like you said, it's insured. Is that insured? Okay, every one of them. You know, one time the FBI said to me, "Hey, lad, you got nice 1.2 million in that store." I started laughing. I go, I got about 800 and some on that store. He started laughing. He goes, "Well, they put in 1.2 million." He didn't care. They they they don't want to. It's done. They they made their money. So I'm going into jewelry store here and got the people down. Two women. They were they were laughing. Actually, the reactions of people are great.

【译】——商业广场（plazas）里的珠宝店，或者更大型的场所，因为我能混进人堆里，能盯梢那家店。如果店开在一家超市里，比如 Publix 商业区，我可以坐在里面看报纸：人们以为我在等店里的人，其实我在观察珠宝店。显然这很花功夫，盯珠宝店的时候你必须高度专注，而且干得越多、手艺越精。接下来显然要开始做计划：什么时候动手、朝哪个方向跑？我跟你说过，如果一家珠宝店门朝东，你就要在早上动手，因为太阳从东边升起，阳光打在玻璃上，谁都看不清里面。你还会看到有的珠宝店把最漂亮的陈列柜摆在正对门口的位置——那简直是为我进店量身定做的完美条件，我爱死了。珠宝店都有门禁蜂鸣器（buzzer），按铃才放你进去。好极了，我就是要这个蜂鸣门。他们想让我进去，因为他们以为能卖给我一枚漂亮的戒指，而别人都进不来。因为有一次十个人一起走进珠宝店，我把他们全按在了地上。再说说你抢的人的反应。首先，被我抢过的人没有一个上过法庭，因为他们都喜欢我。真的，有两位老人说「他抢珠宝店的时候是个好人（nice guy）」。（莱克斯：）因为他们中很多人喜欢你。（拉里：）呃，我从来不会伤害他们。我从不伤害平民，我不信那一套，我不相信伤害无辜、老人或女人。我前面说过了，而且就像你说的——都上着保险呢。（莱克斯：）都上保险？（拉里：）对，每一家都上。有一回 FBI 跟我说：「嘿老兄，你在那家店拿了件好东西，一百二十万美元。」我笑出声，说：那家店我大概拿了八十万多点。他也笑了，说：「嗯，他们申报了一百二十万。」他不在乎。他们不想追究，事情已经了结，他们的钱早到手了。还有一次我进了珠宝店，把人都按倒——两个女的，她们居然在笑。说真的，人的反应太有意思了。

[9000s - 9120s]

Because you know, you think they're going to be some laugh, some. I haven't had any cry, but some just get quiet, and you can see them. I'd say relax. I never would gag anybody because I don't want anybody to throw up and die or any of that nature. I sometimes would blindfold them, but that was it. And usually, I just told them, "Close your eyes, close your eyes, don't move." I always had flex cuffs, you know, like wire ties, flex cuffs. Have them. I, I actually, when I did a speaking engagement in Wisconsin to insurance company, Jewelers Mutual, one of the bigger jewel insurance companies, to all their agents, I literally set my body up to do a robbery. Did an hour speech, brought one of them up on stage, and then jumped them, went crazy, down, get down, everybody in all. I said, "Whoa, I don't have a gun. I had a BB gun, and and and I had the guy, and I said, 'Not one of you guys in here or in this business knew what I was, what what I had on me, did you? Yeah.' And they go, 'Unbelievable! You blended in. You, I had the gun, the the the pillowcases, the gloves, the flex cuffs, everything.' What's the pillowcases for? To bring the jewelry out. That's my bag. Pillowcases. You literally use pillowcases? Yes, literally pillowcases. You don't have to get the nice thread ones, but that's good to know. Cutting costs, but so what I did was this: this guy is complaining, and I'm, I'm thinking, this guy's got to be a cop or something, you know. Now I never robbed the jewelry off the people in the store. You know, if a girl had a ring, I'm not taking a ring. I just didn't do that, and so this guy's kind of getting cocky and talking back. And I, who is this guy? He's got to be a cop or something. So I go up to him, I pull his wallet out. He's nothing. I take his. He had a gold bracelet. It was fake. It was a fagazi, and I pull it off. You piece.

【译】因为你会以为他们会笑出来之类的。我没遇到过谁哭的，但有些人会突然安静下来，你能看得出来。我会说：放轻松。我从来不会堵任何人的嘴，因为我不想让人呕吐窒息而死之类的。我有时会给他们蒙上眼，仅此而已。通常我就直接说：「闭上眼睛，闭上眼睛，别动。」我总是带着束带手铐（flex cuffs），就像扎带那种，给他们戴上。我还有一次，在威斯康星州给一家保险公司——珠宝商互助（Jewelers Mutual），规模最大的珠宝保险公司之一——对他们全体代理人做演讲，我真的是把自己的状态调整成「要去干一票」的样子。讲了一个小时，把其中一位请上台，然后突然扑向他们，疯狂大喊：「趴下！趴下！所有人！」然后我说：「嚯——别怕，我没拿枪，我拿的是 BB 弹枪。」我已经控制住了那个家伙，我说：「这屋里的各位、还有干你们这行的，没有一个人看出来我是什么人、我身上带着什么，对吧？对。」他们说：「难以置信！你完全融入了。你居然身上带着枪、枕套、手套、束带手铐，一应俱全。」（莱克斯：）枕套是干什么用的？（拉里：）把珠宝装出去啊。那就是我的「包」：枕套。（莱克斯：）你真的是用枕套？（拉里：）对，真的是枕套。不用买高支纱的那种，不过这倒是个省钱的窍门。（莱克斯：）削减成本嘛。（拉里：）好，当时是这样的：有个人一直在抱怨，我心里想，这家伙八成是个警察什么的。要知道，我从来不抢店里顾客身上戴的珠宝：比如一个姑娘手上戴着戒指，我不会抢那枚戒指，我就是不干那种事。结果这家伙越来越嚣张，开始顶嘴。我心里想，这人是谁？他肯定是个警察什么的。于是我走上前，把他的钱包抽出来——什么都不是。我又去摘他的东西：他戴着一条金手链，是假的，fagazi（假货），我把它扯了下来。你这坨——

[9120s - 9240s]

Shit! Why are you bothering these girls? They're cracking up, laughing. They didn't like the guy. Yeah. They're cracking. He must bother them all the time. He had a bullshit. They start laughing, and and I ended up walking out. I think there were a total of eight or nine people, ten. I don't know exactly. I ran out of cuffs. I ran out of flex cuffs. I do the legs and feet. I couldn't. I had to just do hands. But nobody left. And I would be. I used to want to be a fly on the wall to find out what happened exactly, how long it took them to get out, how they got out, a whole bunch of stuff. And it just blew me away that you know you've seen so many personalities in there that you know what it is when people are under pressure. Obviously, I think I think pressure is the the best thing a person can go under for whatever it is because your true character, I think your true innovativeness, I think your true looking at things in a different way come out. Obviously, you understand pressure with what you do for a living, but people who don't, they get into these. You know, I think that's just called a rut in life. You know, I don't know how to put yourself under pressure, but talk comedians. I love comedians for that reason. They're under pressure when they get up there. That's not any his job, man. But boy, it shows when they kill it and and they get come more and more confident because it's really more and more pressure you're under. I was good at what I did because I could handle pressure really, and you got to be again. You can't be a moron to start planning. How much time do you have in a store? Like how much time depends. I was one store. I was in one store for 20 minutes because there was a cop waiting out front. A cop car waiting out front. It's just accidentally unrelated to you. Unrelated. I went. It was Daytona, Florida. It's a nice robbery to Daytona Beach, and I go into the store. We take the store down. We have the car waiting in the back and everything. Everybody's down. It was three of us: me and two other guys working. So we had our pillowcases right at the door, ready to go. Well, you look.

【译】我去！你干嘛招惹这几位姑娘？她们都笑翻了。她们不喜欢那家伙。是啊，她们笑个不停。他肯定是老骚扰她们，他那纯属胡闹。她们一笑起来，最后我就走了。我记得店里总共大概八九个人，也可能十个，记不清了。手铐不够用了，束带手铐（flex cuffs）用完了。我平时连腿带脚都铐上，这回不行了，只能铐手。但没有一个人跑掉。我以前常想变成一只趴在墙上的苍蝇（fly on the wall），弄清楚后来到底发生了什么：他们花了多久挣脱、怎么挣脱的，一大堆事。真正让我震撼的是——你在里面见过太多形形色色的人，所以你一眼就能看出人在压力之下是什么样子。我觉得，无论做什么，压力都是一个人能经历的最好的东西，因为你真正的品格、你真正的创造力、你真正换一种方式看问题的能力，都会在压力下显现出来。显然，就你谋生的行当而言，你是懂压力的；可那些不懂的人，就会陷进某种状态里——我想那就叫人生的瓶颈期（rut）吧。我也不知道怎么主动给自己上压力，但看看喜剧演员：我就是因为这个才喜欢喜剧演员，他们一登台就身处压力之下。那可不是份轻松的差事，伙计。可他们一旦炸场（kill it），那股劲头就全出来了，而且会越来越自信，因为你承受的压力只会越来越大。我干我那行干得好，就是因为我特别能扛压，干这行你也必须扛得住。而且要开始做计划，你就不能是个蠢货。——你在一家店里待多久？——要看情况。有一家店，我在里面待了20分钟，因为前门口正好停着一辆警车，有个警察等在那儿。——纯属偶然，跟你无关？——无关。那是在佛罗里达的代托纳比奇（Daytona Beach）干的一票漂亮活儿。我走进店里，把店控制住，车在后门候着，一切安排妥当，所有人都趴下了。我们一共三个人：我和两个一起干活的。枕套就放在门口，随时准备装货走人。结果你瞧。

[9240s - 9360s]

Out the front when you can, just to make sure, because you know if it gets called in right away, some you don't want a guy right there, you know. Oh, Joe, and then you just chasing people. Although I had the car with fake plates and all that kind of stuff done, so waiting, man, we're waiting, and they're getting nervous. Relax, you know, twenty minutes, twenty minutes. This cop was just talking to some driver and nothing. Let's get the fuck out of here, man. I got to get out of here because once I robbed the jewelry store, my jewels were out of my hand within twenty four hours. Incinerated all tags, clothes gone. The the jewels were being melted, and any signature pieces were being taken apart. All gold was being melted, and I am to this day. You're gonna like this. I'm the only man ever to go to prison under the RICO Act. You know what the RICO Act is? Alone. How do you go to prison under the RICO Act alone? The RICO Act is two or more conspired to commit criminals for the furtherance of criminal organization. I went alone. I had a partner, John Rodriguez. Well, there's no John Rodriguez, and John Rodriguez turned out to be my brother, who his wife told on him, and then the government. I am also the only man who was in prison for six years, and I got charged with another federal crime, the same exact crime that Bill Clinton was charged with, 18 USC 1001, filing a false statement. Because I, you know, John Rodriguez. Well, I cost the government over a million dollars in that trial. The judge went nuts, and I ended up getting the time run concurrent because of knowing the law, and I actually won my brother an appeal. Can you explain, John Rodriguez? That wait, Bill Clinton? Well, a second. So you lied. You lied. You lied. Yes. How do you get?

【译】能走前门的时候我们就走前门，只求保险——因为一旦立刻有人报了警，你总不想正好撞上守在那儿的人。哦，Joe，那你就只能被人追着满街跑了。虽然我的车挂着假牌照，该做的都做了。等着，兄弟，我们等啊等，同伙开始紧张了。我说放轻松，就20分钟，20分钟。那个警察不过在跟一个司机说话，没别的事。——赶紧他妈撤，我得离开这儿了——因为我一旦抢完珠宝店，24小时之内货就会出手。所有标签一律烧掉，衣服处理掉；钻石开始熔洗，任何带标志性印记的首饰都被拆散，金子全部熔掉。而直到今天，我仍然是——你们会喜欢这个的——史上唯一一个以 RICO 法案「独自一人」被判入狱的人。你知道 RICO 法案是什么吗？独自一人。一个人怎么会单独以 RICO 法案入狱？RICO 法案的构成是两人或以上合谋犯罪、以助长犯罪组织。而我是单独一个人（被定罪）。我有过一个搭档，约翰·罗德里格斯（John Rodriguez）。其实根本不存在什么约翰·罗德里格斯——约翰·罗德里格斯就是我兄弟，他老婆告发了他，然后政府（顺藤摸瓜）。我还是唯一一个已经蹲了六年监狱又被加控一项联邦罪的人，罪名跟比尔·克林顿被指控的一模一样：18 U.S.C. 1001，提交虚假陈述（filing a false statement）。就是因为约翰·罗德里格斯那档子事——那场审判我让政府花了超过一百万美元，法官气疯了。但因为懂法律，我最终争取到刑期合并执行（concurrent），还真帮我兄弟打赢了一场上诉。——能解释一下约翰·罗德里格斯是怎么回事吗？等等——比尔·克林顿？——稍等一下。——所以你撒谎了？——撒谎了。——你撒谎了？——是的。——那怎么会……

[9360s - 9480s]

How do you get time for lying exactly? Well, when you file a false statement with the federal government, that's a federal crime. So, how are you able? Okay, you said within twenty four hours, melting everything down. How are you not getting caught? How are you able to not get caught for so long? Ah, very good. First of all, nobody, even in my crew, ever gave anything to the people that I gave it to. So there was only one person who could blow this whole thing up. Well, two, me and the guy I dealt with on that end, the fencing end. See, most people do get caught with the fence, so the fence is important. And I dealt with one person who was another family, and when we dealt with each other, it was just I. People might have known, I suspected, but nobody can go into court and say I gave him jewel. He gave me a bag of cash because they would give me a bag of cash for four hundred thousand in in in hundred dollar bills, and I'd go have my pasta in Little Italy. I'd go down to Il Cattil's, eat my pasta, and have fun. And here I got a bag of cash sitting right next to me of four hundred thousand in cash. This is nineteen ninety ninety two. I went from nineteen eighty eight eighty nine to ninety six when I got arrested. So you have a bag of cash in your car, and you go. Yeah, keep it with me. Yeah, no, I keep. He'd pay me. I'd go down, get my money, and come on, let's go across the street. We was there a file on you? I mean, how were you able to? Are you wearing something in your face, or is it always just disguises? Just disguises. I never wore a mask or nothing like that. So I had my line when I went into a different area would be, "Hey, I'm just in the area. I work with FEMA. " And it was you know if there was a disaster anywhere there, yeah, you know I got married ten years ago, and it's coming up on my ten year anniversary. You know, I got my wife a little ring back then. I got her a three quarter diamond. Now I'm looking for a little better. I do well. I'm a builder. If I was a builder, I work with FEMA. I different.

【译】具体说，撒谎怎么就会坐牢？——当你向联邦政府提交虚假陈述时，那就是联邦罪。——那你是怎么做到的？好，你说24小时之内全部熔掉。你怎么没被抓？怎么能这么长时间不被抓住？——啊，问得好。首先，从来没有任何人——连我团伙里的人——都没跟我的下家有过任何交割。所以能把这整件事捅出去的只有一个人。准确说两个：我，还有我在那一头打交道的人——销赃那一头。要知道，大多数人就是栽在销赃人（fence）手里，所以销赃人极其关键。我只跟一个人交易，他属于另一个家族；我们之间打交道，从来都是我单线联系。也许有人知情——我猜有——但没有任何人能上法庭说「我给了他珠宝、他给了我现金」。因为他们付给我的是一袋现金：40万美元，全是一百的票子，然后我就去小意大利（Little Italy）吃我的意面，到 Il Cattil's 那家馆子，吃面，找乐子，身边座位上就搁着一袋40万美元的现金。那是1990、1992年时候的事。我从1988、1989年一路干到1996年被捕。——所以你车里就放着一袋现金，然后就……——对，随身带着，我一直带着。他付钱，我过去取钱，然后「走，去街对面」。——他们那儿存了你的档案吗？我是说，你是怎么做到的？你脸上戴什么吗，还是全靠伪装？——全靠伪装。我从来不戴面罩之类的东西。所以每到一个新地方，我都有固定说辞：「嗨，我就是来这片办事的，我在 FEMA（联邦应急管理署）工作。」而且要是那片区域正好闹过灾害，嘿，那就更对路了。接着就是：「我十年前结的婚，马上十周年了。当年给太太买过一枚小戒指，四分之三克拉的钻，现在想换颗好一点的。我混得不错，我是搞建筑的承包商。」——是承包商嘛，跟 FEMA 有工作往来，这套说辞就立住了，各种变着来。

[9480s - 9600s]

Lines, and I said, "Now, and I did that because I wanted the guy to break out the big box, the the real money box. If the guy looked at me and says, 'Oh, let me show you a couple of these,' you know, this what you're going to show me? I'm looking to, you know, I'm looking to spend about ten grand. I'm just looking around, but if you're good, if you got a good price, oh, really? Hold on. He would break out a box. Now I know he's a wholesaler. I know what I want. I kind of knew he was just by the the what he does, what he sells, and stuff. So now he breaks out that box I was telling you about. That's that long. I'd say, yeah, let me see a carat first, a nice carat. He would pull up envelope. Now, depending where he pulled on that end box, so let's say the box is this long, and he pulls over here, he'd show me this. I'm interested, and then I go, let me see, carat and a half. He'd put that back. If he goes this way, I know these are all gone, but you know, little or then again, or they're not. But if he goes this way and only here, I I used to be able to literally say, okay, that's a quarter million. I could take that. That court. That's a quarter million. Now, clean, a clean box, because they were just loose stones. And now I I'd have them. You know, the reason I tell say that the business is so dirty, they would try to con me. And I went to the GIA Institute. They would never. I often tell, I'll tell all your audience, if you want to go buy a diamond, if you do not see a 10 powered microscope on the counter, walk out. If the guy doesn't show you what they call a diamond light box, and it doesn't tell you about it, leave. Here's why. I can show you a one carat diamond in three ways. The same weight of diamond will be one will be this way and short, and this way, one will be perfect cut about.

【译】……就这么一套套台词。我说这些，是因为我要让店主把那个大盒子请出来——真正值钱的盒子。要是店主看着我说「哦，我给您看几款这个」，我就来一句：「就给我看这些？我可是打算花上一万美金左右的。我只是先看看，不过你要是价格给力——」「哦，是吗？您稍等。」他就会去搬盒子。到这一步我就知道他是做批发的，也知道我要什么了。其实光凭他做的东西、卖的东西，我早就有数了。于是他搬出我跟你说过的那种盒子，有这么长。我说：「行，先看一克拉的，一颗漂亮的一克拉。」他就抽出一个信封。关键看他从盒子哪个位置往外抽。假设盒子有这么长：他从这一头抽，给我看这档，我装作有兴趣，说「再看看一克拉半的」，他就把方才的放回去；如果他往那一头翻，我就知道那边的货都出得差不多了——也可能没有，说不准。但要是他只固定从这一个位置抽，我几乎能脱口而出：好，这一格是25万美元的货，我可以吃下。就那个位置——那就是25万。而且是一整箱干净的货，全是散钻（loose stones），这下货就到手了。我说这一行脏，就脏在这儿——连他们都想坑我，可我是上过美国宝石学院（GIA）的人，他们坑不了。我常跟人说——也告诉你所有的观众——你要去买钻石，如果柜台上看不到一台十倍放大镜（10x），扭头就走；如果店家不给你看他们所谓的钻石灯箱（diamond light box）、也不给你讲解，走人。原因是：同样一颗一克拉钻石，我能给你摆出三种样子。同样重量，有的切得又扁又矮，有的这样那样，其中只有一颗是完美切工，大约……

[9600s - 9720s]

Fifty six percent, they call it fifty six degree. Here goes this one, and it's perfect. And here is why: a diamond reflects light. It hits the diamond; it's all geometry. Hits the diamond, goes across, comes up, bam! It blows in your face. Well, if the diamond's not cut the right way, it goes down, out, or down, up. It goes the wrong way, and they don't want to show you. The first thing they'll do is look at how beautiful that is, and they'll show you under a light. Get out of here! Take your diamond. If you have somebody who has a diamond, and they say, "Oh, take the diamond. Go like this under the desk," and if it's still cut, then you know it's cut right because it's going to take whatever little light is there and reflect it the right way. And it goes with all. It's all about geometry and how they cut them. That's why cutters are worth so much money. But most people will go to one person for a diamond. Did you know a diamond will be the third largest purchase you will ever make? It's a house, a car, and a diamond. Now, most people go to a guy who cons them and tells them all of this shit. Take that information and go to the next guy. You should go to four guys and get as many recommendations as you can. Oh, I'm going to give you a guarantee. I'll buy it back. I'll do all this shit. They're car salesmen with diamonds, but there are obviously diamonds are worth money. I don't know about investments, of course, but it's worth money. And again, I'm not one of these gaudy people. I think a woman with the perfect size, a petite woman has carat earrings. They're too big or whatever. They have to be petite. They have to be looking right. They have to enhance the beauty of the person instead of just you know gaudy. I call it. You know, that's just my taste, and I think most people's taste. What? So you said you're doing the research there on the terms of in terms of what kind of gems they have. What about other things? I would.

【译】56%，他们管它叫56度。这一颗就是，完美。道理在这儿：钻石反射光线。光进入钻石，全凭几何——打进去，横穿，再折上来，砰！直晃你眼。可要是切工不对，光就往下漏、往外漏，或者上下乱走，走错方向——所以他们不想让你看。他们的第一招就是「您看多漂亮」，然后拿到灯底下给你看。得了吧，带着你的钻石一边去！要是有店家跟你说「来，把钻石拿到桌子底下这么看看」，它在那么暗的地方还照样闪，你就知道切工是对的，因为它能抓住仅有的那点微光，朝正确的方向反射。全都一个道理：钻石就是几何学，全看怎么切。所以切工师才那么值钱。可大多数人买钻石只进一家店。你知道吗，钻石会是你一生第三大的支出：房子、汽车，然后就是钻石。大多数人遇到的都是能说会道坑他的卖家，听他白话一堆，再拿着这些信息去下一家。你该跑四家店，尽可能多收集参考意见。「我给你保证，以后可以回购」，一套一套的屁话。他们就是拿着钻石的二手车推销员。当然，钻石显然值钱——是不是好投资我不敢说，但它值钱。另外，我不是那种喜欢俗气大件的人。我觉得尺寸恰到好处才最美。一位娇小的女士戴一克拉的耳环——太大了，怎么都不合适。首饰必须精致，必须看着协调，必须衬托人的美，而不是俗气扎眼——我管那叫「土豪气」。当然这只是我的品味，我想也是大多数人的品味。——等等，你说你在那边做调研，是摸清他们有什么样的宝石。那其他方面呢？——我会（去摸）……

[9720s - 9840s]

Know who's coming, who's going, what cars they're at, what cars they own, the employees, yes, the employees and the neighbors, the neighbor of that place. I would know when the mailman comes, when any packages are delivery, what security is in the area, what when the cops come around, if it's a shift time, some cops have different shifts, so you know if the cops come on shift that they first come to to that area. When the trash is picked up in the back, where usually your car is, you have to know. There's so many robberies I did that were just because of smart. Like this one robbery out in Coral Springs, Florida. Here, this guy would come in every day, and he opened at ten, but at nine thirty, right before he'd open the safe, get ready, clean out the garbage, and before he put everything out in the in the in the shelves, he would come take the trash out in the back. After enough time knowing his routine, I literally sat against the wall. His door's right here in the back door. I hear the door opening. He opens the door. I go in, shove him, fly him down in the back. Don't move! Don't move! Turn him over! Boom! Boom! Clap him up. Let my partner in. We were in and out there in probably a minute, minute and a half, because it was in the safe. Everything we knew was right where it was, and left. Goodbye. Done. So, how did you get caught? I got caught by good police work. I did a robbery in Fairless Hills, Pennsylvania. Little did I know when I was in the area, I cased probably over a thousand stores in my career, a thousand. That's why obviously insurance companies or police they they can get a lot of knowledge from what I do. So when I cased a lot of stores, I was in the area. Fairless, I cased a store.

【译】……摸清谁进谁出，他们开什么车、名下有什么车；店里的员工——对，员工——还有周边的邻居，那家店一带的邻居。我要弄清邮差几点来，什么时候有包裹投递，这一带有什么安保，警察什么时候巡逻、是不是交接班时段——有些警察班次不同，你得知道他们上岗后头一站会不会巡到那片。后院的垃圾几点收，你的车一般停哪儿——这些都必须门清。我干的那么多票，很多赢就赢在脑子好使。比如佛罗里达珊瑚泉（Coral Springs）那一票。那家伙每天来开店，十点开门，但九点半——就在他开保险柜、做准备、清垃圾、往货架摆货之前——他会先把垃圾拿到后门外头。把他的规律摸得足够透之后，我就真的贴墙坐在他后门口。他的后门就在那儿。我听见门响，他一开门，我冲进去，一把把他掀翻在后屋。「别动！别动！」把他翻过来，咔、咔，铐上，放我搭档进来。前后大概一分到一分半我们就撤了，因为东西全在保险柜里，一切如我们预判，拿了就走。拜拜，收工。——那你是怎么落网的？——我栽在扎实的警察工作上。我在宾州费尔利斯希尔斯（Fairless Hills）干了一票。我当时并不知道——我职业生涯里踩点（casing）过的店恐怕超过一千家，一千家啊。所以保险公司和警察显然能从我这里挖到很多东西。我踩了那么多店，人就在那一带活动。费尔利斯那边，我踩过一家店……

[9840s - 9960s]

Probably three, four miles away from the store I eventually robbed. Well, the person in that store got my plate number because they wanted to sell me a ring, so they they knew somebody. Because you know, I do my whole spiel and find out if they have the right jewels. It's one of the parts of how I do it. Is I got to then after I pick the right location, I know I can get away with it. It's facing the right direction. It's close enough. They have a right back exit. There's no one around. You can get away. Whatever the system is, then the next step is to go into the store to make sure they have the right kind of jewelry, right kind of setup. And I often tell people the single best thing you can do to stop a professional like me is have a two way glass, or even say it's two way glass, because if if I don't know, then I'm not going to rob that store. Also, there's a lot of other things because I used to take the the video cameras, the equipment, right out of the store. Today they should be offline. They should be into a cloud of some sort, so you can't you know just get them the footage. But anyway, so I go into the store and they wanted to sell me a ring, so they got my plate. Little did I know at this time there was a guy named Matt Mullin, FBI agent. No hate, nice guy. Never tried to do anything wrong. He said he was retiring after he caught me. He was looking for me for so long. He goes, "You are the best I've ever seen do it." And he was not like after me. He wasn't like, "Oh yeah, you're a scumbag" or anything. He was a nice guy, FBI agent, and he says they had a task force that when a robbery of my mo happened, they would flood an area with agents. They would confiscate cameras from the Wawa stores, from the Seven Elevens. They got me buying a cup of coffee at a convenience store. Oh, really? Interesting. So they placed. They were thorough. They oh, when they came to my hearing, they had a they.

【译】……离我最终抢的那家店大概三四英里。那家店里的人记下了我的车牌号，因为他们想把一枚戒指卖给我，就托了认识的人去打听。要知道，我会把整套戏码从头演到尾，摸清他们有没有对路的货，这是我作案流程的一环。流程是这样：我先选对位置——确认能得手、能脱身：朝向对、距离够近、有合适的后门出口、周围没人、撤得掉。这些系统性的条件都齐了，下一步就是进店，确认他们有对路的珠宝、对路的格局。我常跟人说，要防住我这种职业选手，最管用的一招就是装双向玻璃（two way glass）——甚至只要嘴上说装了就行，因为我摸不清，就不会抢那家店。另外还有很多别的门道，比如我以前会把监控摄像头、整套设备直接从店里搬走。如今监控都该走云端存储，存到某种云上，你就没法顺手把录像端走了。总之，我进了那家店，他们想卖我戒指，于是拿到了我的车牌。我当时并不知道，有个叫马特·马林（Matt Mullin）的 FBI 探员正在办我的案子。没有怨恨，他是个好人，从没使过下三滥的手段。他说抓到我之后就退休。他找了我那么久。他说：「你是我见过的干这行最出色的。」他追我，但不是那种「你就是个人渣」的路数；他是个正派的 FBI 探员。他说他们组了专案组，只要发生与我作案手法（MO）相符的抢劫案，他们就会往那一带撒满探员。他们调取 Wawa、7-Eleven 这些便利店的监控，拍到了我在便利店买咖啡。——哦，是吗？有意思。——所以说他们布了控，他们非常彻底。等到出席我的听证会时，他们推来了……

We had a a hand truck, you know, full of boxes of investigation. That's how long they were after me. And I asked them. Then they they leave them because they give them a little hard time. He goes, the insurance guy. He goes, the people. They made money. He goes, they didn't. They did not hate you. Come over here. Sit here in the chair. Just look that way. Didn't tie them up. Didn't do it. Now, just look that way. And when I leave, you have to wait a few minutes, and then you can get up and go. The next day in the paper, they said, "Oh, he was a nice man. I was the nice guy. I got that store for near a million bucks, but I was a nice guy that night. I didn't want. I really didn't hurt people. I mean, my goal was. I would have gotten caught before I hurt somebody in there. That wasn't my intent. Again, I would never hurt anybody who wasn't in my business. If you were in my business, a little different. You were drug dealer trying to do something. You were bookie trying to get. You were robbing something. I once dragged a kid who robbed my bike down a street. Nobody ever robbed my bike again. You know, in my neighborhood, right down the street, I robbed, dragged the kid. I paid five hundred bucks to have somebody go find out who robbed that bike. Bike was worth five hundred dollars, but I made an example so nobody robbed in our neighborhood. Well, that's a code. That's a code to live by. It is as much as it might be a little messed up in my head. Actually, I don't think you've said just that moment of the first day in prison. That process of being.

【译】……一辆手推车，装满了整箱整箱的调查案卷。他们追了我就是这么久。我问过他们，他们说：「拉里，老百姓并不恨你——你知道谁恨你吗？保险公司。他们天天催着我们的上司去抓你，因为你让保险公司亏惨了。首先，他们赔的是真金白银；而人人都想多捞好处，他们试着（涨钱）收钱，又因为给人添堵，把客户弄丢了。」——他是说，保险那帮人照样赚钱；至于老百姓？他们还赚了钱（拿到理赔）。「他们不恨你，真的不恨你。」我在佛罗里达萨拉索塔（Sarasota）抢过一家店，有一对老夫妇走了进来。他们一进来，我说：这是抢劫。我穿着运动西装，亮出枪。哦不不不——我说，别紧张，过来，坐这把椅子上，朝那边看就行。没绑他们，什么都没做，就让他们朝那边看着。等我走了，你们等几分钟再起来走动。第二天的报纸上，他们说：「哦，他是个好人。」我成了那个「好人」。那家店我拿了将近一百万美元的货，但那天晚上我确实当了个好人。我不想伤人，我真的没伤过人。我的目标是——我宁可先被抓，也不想在店里伤到人，那从来不是我的本意。再说一遍：不在这一行里的人，我绝不会伤害。你要是在道上，那另说——你是毒贩想搞事，你是收注庄家（bookie）想捞一把，你在抢别人的东西，那另当别论。有一次，一个孩子偷了我的自行车，我把他拖着在大街上拖了一路，从此再没人偷过我的车。就在我家那条街上。我花500美元雇人去查出是谁偷的车。那车也就值500美元，但我要杀一儆百，让我们那一带从此没人敢偷东西。——这算一种规矩，一条做人的守则。——是的。虽然在我脑子里，这套规矩可能有点扭曲。其实你还没讲过——进监狱第一天的那个时刻，那整套（脱衣搜身）的流程……

I've heard you criticize, sort of not criticize, but in a friendly way, describe like the Mr. Beast video about spending time in prison. And you mentioned that the actual sort of you going through the process of getting stripped and searched and all this kind of stuff. Can you take me that process? It's first of all, there's a lot of demeaning. It's part of what they do. See, there's two types of prisons. Is when you first get arrested, is one one thing. You are still in shock. First of all, you're probably in shock for two years. What do you mean? You don't get comfortable in a prison setting, a real prison setting, approximately two years. Especially when you get, you know, you're doing a long time, and you know, you're going to a very violent place. You you're looking over your shoulder. You're figuring out who's your friend and foe. You you have to, you know, judge a room. You're talking about reading a room. You better read a room well. And what I mean when you see people walking around with their boots, and you see a person with a magazine in their under their clothes, you're ready for you're ready for a war because they use that as body armor. You put a magazine between your, you know, your shirt and your pants. And you know, you start feeling. You know, you get to the point where you're really good. I could feel tension. If I felt threatened now, I'd you know I'd be. I feel very comfortable. And if you're not, hairs go up on your head. You know when something's going to happen, and it's it's amazing. But a person, the demeaning part, or you know, stripped naked, lift your nuts, turn around, spread your ass, do every single day. I mean, they, you know, then they'll put you in a cell. They call it if they think you're smuggling drugs. They'll put you in what they call a dry cell. You got shit in a bucket, and there's no water, no cleaning, no the demeaning things that happen for the you know the prison system has a great line to get away with a lot. It's for the safe and orderly running of the institution where.

【译】我听你评论过——不算批评，挺友善地评过 Mr. Beast 那期体验坐牢的视频。你也提到过你自己真正经历的那一套：被脱光、被搜身，等等。能带我过一遍那个流程吗？首先，那里面全是羞辱，这是他们行事的一部分。要知道，监狱分两种情形。刚被捕是一种情形，那会儿人还在震荡期。首先，你大概有两年都在震荡中。——此话怎讲？——在真正的监狱环境里——是真监狱——你大约要两年才能适应。尤其当你刑期很长，而且要去的是个非常暴力的地方。你得时刻回头提防，分辨谁是敌谁是友，你得学会判断一个屋子的气氛——就是你说的「读懂房间」（read a room），你最好读得准准的。我什么意思？当你看到有人蹬着靴子来回走，看到有人衣服底下塞着杂志，那就是准备开战了，因为杂志是拿来当护甲的：把杂志垫在衬衫和裤子之间。慢慢地你会有感觉，练到很深的时候，我对气氛极其敏感，我能感觉到张力。要是我现在觉得受威胁，我也会……（但如今）我心里很踏实。你要是不敏感，你后颈的汗毛会竖起来。你就是知道要出事了，这很神。至于羞辱的部分——脱得精光，抬起蛋蛋，转身，掰开屁股，每天来这么一套。然后他们会把你关进一间牢房：要是怀疑你体内藏毒，就把你关进所谓的「干监室」（dry cell）——你只能对着一个桶排泄，没有水，没有清洁，什么都没有。监狱系统干这些羞辱人的事，还总有一句万能的挡箭牌：「为了本机构的安全与有序运行」……

[10200s - 10320s]

Stopping recreation today, for the safe and orderly running of the institution, no food for lunch. They can do that, and they do do it. So you learn real quick. Survival is part of, you know. I would eat. I go from being a millionaire to going to prison and eating twenty-five cent ramen noodle soups raw, and then drinking water so they expand in your stomach, and you're not hungry. So the swing of life is it's a test. Some don't make it. I still believe whatever you do survive makes you so much. You know, I often tell people, "Why do you work so hard? I love what I do, and but but Lair, what? I lived in an eight foot by six foot cell. You think I give a fuck? And they go, "Good point. Yeah, everything's a plus. There's no negatives. So what's food like in prison? Who, who, you had to go there. Food is garbage. Okay. Put it this way: I'm lucky I had resources, money, because i I would, you know, do a protein push by eating a jar of peanut butter every day, and I would get buy chicken from the from you know kitchen workers and everything I can to get as much protein as I can because they it's not they're going to feed you with starches and and carbs that'll just kill you. Trust me, I didn't eat. I ate the way I had to. The ramen noodles was a hunger thing. You when you hear your stomach growl, you everybody's heard their stomach growl. Well, when you hear it for real, what uh, what food were you fantasizing about when you were in prison? Oh, wow, yes. You know, I'm I'm a meat eater, steak eater. I love a perfect.

【译】「即日起停止放风，为保障本机构安全有序运行，午餐不供餐食。」他们真能这么干，而且真干。所以你很快就学乖了，生存本身就是（监狱里的头等课）。我从百万富翁沦为囚犯，生吃25美分一包的方便面（ramen），然后灌水，让面在胃里泡发，就不饿了。人生这种大起大落就是一场考验，有人没扛过去。但我始终相信，你在其中活下来所经历的一切，都会让你强大得多。我常跟人说：「你干吗这么拼？」——因为我热爱我做的事。「可是拉里，怎么说？我可是住过8英尺乘6英尺牢房的人，你觉得我还在乎个屁？」对方就说：「有道理。是啊，一切都是加分项，没有减分项。」——那监狱里的伙食到底怎么样？——嚯，你还真问到这儿了。伙食就是垃圾。这么说吧：幸亏我有门路有钱。我会搞「蛋白质突击」——每天干掉一整罐花生酱，还从厨房干活的囚犯那儿买鸡肉，想尽一切办法多摄入蛋白质。因为监狱不会给你这些，他们喂你的是淀粉和碳水，那种东西能把人毁掉。相信我，我不是嘴馋，我是按需要吃。方便面是为了扛饿。肚子咕咕叫谁都听过；可当你真的饿得肚子咕咕叫……——那你在狱里最馋的是什么吃的？——哦，哇，好问题。我是吃肉的人，牛排爱好者，我最爱一块完美的……

[10320s - 10440s]

Steak, it kind of melts in my mouth. So I always used to say, "I want a twenty-four ounce steak," and you know how big that is. Now I can't even eat that. You know, I mean, I'll take a nice twelve, you know, twelve ounce steak. Again, I notice it's crazy. Food drives me nuts. To this day, I could like I have a dish at home waiting for me that I made with. It's got pasta, it's got chicken, it's got bacon, chips, and it's got cheese, and it's like a creamy cheese. And it it was so good. I'm learning something better. The older I get, I make a lot. I used to eat it all. I got to three o' five once. No more. Let me ask you about. We kind of touched on it, but race and gangs. Like, what are the groups in prison? Oh wow, I'll just give some. Okay, let's try. Let's go right to the gate. The white boy, the dirty white boys, Aryan brother, Aryan circle, Aryan nation. Those are your your main white gangs. Aryan Brotherhood is one of the bad. You know, I put up a video about the baddest prison gangs, the Aryan Brotherhood, and oh, the video went crazy because people, oh no, it's the Mexican Mafia. No, it's the listen, Aryan Brotherhood. They will cut your head off. Most of them are alive. They're the brand they call themselves as well. It's called the brand, and have the shamrock, and they, you know, listen. I've been in the prison with these guys. Most people are people at first, but of course, you know, the gangs came about because whites are very minority in prison. They were actually founded in the California prison system, the Aryan Brotherhood. But then Puerto Ricans, Latin Kings, Netas, and then Mexicans. You have Border Brothers, Mexican Mafia, EME, the Sureños, Nortenos. They're different gangs from California and blacks. You know the Black Guerrilla Family, the BGF.

【译】……牛排，入口即化的那种。所以我那时候总说：「我要一份24盎司的牛排。」你知道那有多大。现在我根本吃不下那份量了，来一份漂亮的12盎司就很好。而且我发现挺不可思议——食物能让我着迷成这样，到今天都是。我家里现在还等着一道我自己做的菜：有意面、鸡肉、培根、薯片，还有奶酪，奶油质地的那种，好吃极了。我还在学更好的做法。年纪越大越会做，我一次做很多。以前我能全吃光，有一回（体重）干到305（磅），再也不行了。让我问问这个——我们多少碰过一点——种族与帮派。监狱里都有哪些圈子？——哦，哇，我就随便说几个。好，试试吧。从大门说起：白人这边，「脏白仔」（dirty white boys）、雅利安兄弟会（Aryan Brotherhood）、雅利安圈（Aryan Circle）、雅利安国度（Aryan Nation），这些是主要的白人帮派。雅利安兄弟会是其中最狠的之一。我发过一期讲最狠监狱帮派的视频，讲到雅利安兄弟会，视频爆了，因为有人非说：「不对，是墨西哥黑手党。」不是的，听我说——雅利安兄弟会，他们能把你的头砍下来。他们大多是终身监禁。他们还自称「品牌」（the brand），标志是三叶草。听着，我跟这些人在同一所监狱里待过。大多数人一开始也就是普通人。当然，这些帮派之所以成气候，是因为白人在监狱里是极少数。雅利安兄弟会就是在加州监狱系统里创立的。再往下是波多黎各人：拉丁国王（Latin Kings）、Netas；墨西哥人：Border Brothers、墨西哥黑手党（Mexican Mafia，即 EME）、Sureños、Norteños——都是源自加州的不同帮派。黑人这边，有黑色游击家族（Black Guerrilla Family，BGF）……

[10440s - 10560s]

And then you have a Bloods, Crips, DC Boys. DC Boys were crazy. You know they closed the DC jails and they put them all in the federal system. Since they did that, the system went pretty crazy because all of DC is federal. So they become federal prisoners. You could rob a, you know, Seven Eleven and go to federal prison. And federal prison is known more for the bigger criminals. The, you know, I, you know, it's funny. I was talking. I was going to a a guy's show. It's in a rough neighborhood. I said, "Really?" I says, "I lived in a place where everybody was a murderer, a hit man, a mafia boss. Trust me, I'm not worried, and I'm not. Not that I'm tough, because that ain't gonna happen. Anybody, you know that. It's just it doesn't fear me. I mean, I lived in New York. I know what Harlem was. I knew, and I, and again, I'm not a racist person. Period. Zero. I treat you how you treat me, and that goes for anybody. It's interesting you say Aryan Brotherhood was dangerous. What, what are they really racially? Like, is there an ideology? Do they all? Oh, absolutely. No, no, they have they have a code. They have they literally have a a way and things they have to follow. Matter of fact, Sylvester is a guy was the founder, one of the founder. Did you know he was the only inmate was he was in the hole for thirty years? Talk about crazy. I know guys who were doing his mail, and they said he. You want to talk about demonic shit? You know, and you got to look up Sylvester. He killed a guard, and one of the black gangs, he killed their guy, and he killed a guard, and it's the son of the guard, and he he was in what they call no human contact for twenty nine or thirty years since I think nineteen eighty s. So, and he didn't die not long ago. But anyway, I saw his cell because they had a cell for him in Atlanta, and he was underneath the movie theater where it was old movie theater, and to see how they set it up.

【译】然后还有血帮（Bloods）、瘸帮（Crips）、DC Boys。DC Boys 疯得很。你知道，他们把华盛顿特区的监狱关了，把囚犯全塞进了联邦系统。自从这一改，整个系统就乱套了，因为 DC 全境归联邦，这些人全成了联邦囚犯——你抢个 7-Eleven 都能进联邦监狱。而联邦监狱本来是以大案要犯闻名的地方。说个好玩的：有一次我要去看一个朋友的演出，那地方治安很差。我说：「真的吗？」我说：「我住过的地方，人人都是杀人犯、职业杀手、黑帮老大。相信我，我不怕，我确实不怕。」倒不是我多能打——那种事根本不会发生，谁都清楚。只是我心里没那根恐惧的弦。我在纽约住过，我知道哈莱姆什么样，我了解。再说一遍，我不是种族主义者，完全没有，零。你怎么对我，我就怎么对你，对谁都一样。——你说雅利安兄弟会危险，有意思。他们在种族上到底是什么？有意识形态吗？是不是人人都信？——哦，当然。不不，他们有自己的一套规矩，真的有一条必须遵循的路数和章程。说真的，西尔维斯特（Sylvester）是创始人之一。你知道吗，他是唯一一个在「洞」里关了三十年的囚犯？什么叫疯狂。我认识替他收发信件的人，他们说他的事——你想听听什么叫恶魔行径吗？你自己去查查西尔维斯特。他杀过一名狱警，还杀过某个黑人帮派的人，又杀了那名狱警的儿子，然后被投入所谓的「无人接触」（no human contact）监禁，一关就是29还是30年，大概从1980年代起。不久前他才去世。总之，我见过他的牢房——在亚特兰大那所监狱，他们专门给他留了一间，位置在一座老电影院的底下。看看他们把那间牢房布置成什么样……

[10560s - 10680s]

It's it's it boggles the mind how they treated that man. Then they have biker gangs. Obviously, the outlaws, the Hell's Angels, they're in there too as well. But you know, you'll find that most of the gangs, if anybody tells you they were in a medium prison, they see the area, brother, they're not because they don't go to medium security prison. They're all murderers. They're all most of them a life. If they get out on appeal, I mean, they really control the outside as well, and that's why they're intimidating the guards too. They have a whole network on the outside, and it's funny, Lex, because you're going to see them work with black groups. You're going to see them working with Mexican groups or Spanish. It doesn't matter. You have to. You know, we you can't just be alone in prison, especially a prison on the yard. They hang with themselves. They play cards themselves. They work out together. The EME are amazingly tight. That's the Mexican Mafia. They will work out sick workouts. That's how I got into the workouts with them, and and they will, and they're doing that ready for war. I mean, they come up with that routine, and I hope you try that routine. Did you have to choose? Like with all these gangs, did you have to? I I got into the law. I was, you know, I by being into the law so deep. I mean, I'm in the law library. I did favors for people. I had connections on the street with a lawyer, so I can get people's paperwork. So I ended up working with the shot callers for the gangs. The shot callers are the people who run the gangs. Now, when you can do that, I mean, they listen. They say, "Oh, we'll pay you." You you got me. Whatever you want, whatever you want, and I would when I pull a guy's paperwork, you know, they knew because I I didn't do this. Oh, it's good. Go get them. I didn't want that. Here's what it is. This is not lying. I used to justify myself when people got messed up pretty good, and they got. I watched a guy get kicked in the head so hard with steel. Not I don't know if it was steel. It was steel.

【译】他们那样对待那个人，简直匪夷所思。然后还有飞车党：显然有 Outlaws（亡命之徒）、地狱天使（Hell's Angels），里面也有这些人。但你会发现，大多数（硬核）帮派——要是有谁跟你说他在中等设防监狱里见过这些帮派，兄弟，不可能，因为他们不去中等设防监狱。他们全是杀人犯，大多数判的是终身，除非上诉翻案出来。他们实际上还控制着外面的世界，这也是狱警都怵他们的原因。他们在外面有整套网络。有意思的是，莱克斯，你会看到他们跟黑人团伙合作，也会跟墨西哥裔或西语裔团伙合作——无所谓，必须这样。在监狱里你不能孤身一人，尤其是在大院里。他们各帮各扎堆，自己人打牌，一起锻炼。EME 抱团紧得吓人，那就是墨西哥黑手党。他们的训练狠得要命——我就是这么跟他们练到一块儿的。他们练那些就是在备战。那套训练法是他们琢磨出来的，我希望你也去试试那套动作。——那你必须选边站吗？这么多帮派，你非得选吗？——我走的是钻研法律这条路。因为钻得深，我整天泡在法律图书馆，帮人办事，外面又有律师关系，能搞到别人的案卷文书（paperwork）。于是我就跟各帮派的话事人（shot caller）打上了交道——话事人就是帮派里管事的人。你有这个本事，他们就会听你的，说：「我们会付你钱。」「你帮了我大忙，想要什么都行。」我去调某人案卷的时候，他们心里有数，因为我从不干那种事——看了卷宗说「好，去收拾他」——我不要那种（结果）。实情是这样的，我不撒谎：每当有人被修理得很惨，我以前总要给自己找理由开脱……我亲眼看见一个人被钢头靴踢中头部。不——我不确定是不是钢头的。就是钢头的。

【译】……那些靴子一下下砸在他头上，每踢中一下，就溅起六英尺长的血线。他们不停地踢、不停地踢。听着，那人是个十足的败类。让我同情一个欺负六岁孩子的人，我做不到。有些人的日子就该过得艰难——像他那种人就活该不好过，不应该让他舒坦。所以我心里有一部分赞成对这种人严惩。但显然，我也信改造重生。可现实里存在两套司法系统，这一点让我挺窝火：一套给请得起顶级律师的人，一套给请不起的人——这种事我不认。请错了律师，就是终身监禁；请对了律师，压根不用坐牢。干着一模一样事情的另一个人却在蹲大牢。干脆承认实情就好——你要是坦白承认，人家进来一看，没什么可争的，那我认。如今我自己占系统的便宜了，没关系。但别标榜自己更高贵，或说他们干了这种事就只是人渣。监狱里虽关着人，可里面有很多好人，也有一堆人渣；高墙外面同样既有好人也有人渣。——你提到过，你对怎么修复监狱系统可能有些想法。——我有个办法，能很快治住监狱里的暴力和腐败，而且不花钱，零成本。首先，监狱的问题——大家都自以为懂——真正的问题是那些法律：因为鸡毛蒜皮的小事就把人关进去，这必须停止。修复监狱系统最好的办法：他们有个东西叫 ACA，他称之为 American Accreditation Association（美国惩教协会，ACA）。想听听什么叫扯淡吗，莱克斯？他们来到一所……

Prison, and they say, "Oh, we're an ACA approved prison." Really, they call them two weeks beforehand. Say, "We're going to come inspect your prison." You better let these people in because you know a United States senator cannot go to a prison right now and go in and inspect that. 【译】……监狱，然后说：「哦，我们是 ACA 认证监狱。」是吗？他们提前两周打电话：「我们要来检查贵监了。」你知道他们对关在「洞」里的刺头囚犯做了什么吗？我亲眼看着人在「洞」里疯掉——真的疯：用粪便在墙上写恶魔的字句，朝人扔粪便和尿液——朝狱警或者随便什么人。监狱里尽是这种病态的事，因为人在发疯。你不能认为这是正常的运作方式。ACA 的运作方式是：来到监狱，检查伙食。ACA 来的时候我们可爱坏了，因为那几天吃得像样，该办的事都办了，坏了很久的东西修好了，各种好事都来了。但这就是扯淡，因为他们前脚一走——跟你说，我住的地方有一所监狱，本地监狱，拿了认证。他们通风系统的滤网上贴着日期标签，只在检查时才换。也就是说，没检查的时候，连空气都没过滤。连这都如此，我们还怎么相信这些人能把别的事做对？可行的办法是：我可以组一支队伍——像我这样的人、一名狱警、一位前典狱长、一位在里面干过的医务人员，也许再加一位前行政人员——拥有完整授权，可以进入任何一所监狱，比如联邦系统的，任何监狱、任何时候。谁敢拦着不让我们进，拦的人当场开除：养老金没了，一切都没了。这种事只要发生一次，消息传开：你最好放这帮人进去。要知道，现在的美国参议员都进不了监狱去检查。100位最有权势的人——535位参众议员：100位参议员、435位众议员……

They cannot go into any prison and inspect that prison unless they get clearance. It takes weeks. They have to be notified. That's not an inspection. That's bullshit. I'm one of the only prisoners who went back to his own prison and actually spoke at a pre-release class, and it was amazing. I'm walking on the yard with the warden and the captain. I go, this is freaking surreal. I'm walking like no, because they have movements. You know, you can't just move in a prison. They have what they call ten minute moves. So they'll say, "Uh, ten minute move, ten minute move." So at one o'clock, they say ten minute move. You have ten minutes. You leave your unit. You go wherever you're going to go. After the move is closed, the prison is shut down. So if you go to the yard, great, go to yard. You can't leave that yard till the next move. You go to library. You can't move that yard. Whatever you go, education or wherever it is, you can't leave that facility. That

【译】……不经批准，他们进不了任何一所监狱去检查。批下来要几个星期，还得提前通知。那不叫检查，那是扯淡。组建一支队伍，这支队伍有权在周日晚上九点直接走进那所监狱。至于为什么要带上我这样的人，而不只是狱警——因为我知道怎么跟囚犯交谈，让他们说出真话；他们不会对另一个狱警说实话，因为他们怕转头就挨收拾。我甚至有办法防止他们事后被报复：设一套回拨系统，从办公室直接回访本人，核实他们有没有受虐待、有没有被调走。因为他们的惯用手法是：把那个在「洞」里疯闹的囚犯——你知道检查期间怎么处理吗？把他挪到最尽头的监区层（tier），直接给他注射 Thorazine（氯丙嗪），让他闭嘴。然后就说：「看，我们检查过了。」队伍里要有我这样的人，因为我知道该去哪儿。莱克斯，我现在就派你和一帮人进那所监狱查个明白，你根本不知道去哪儿、找谁聊、里面的门道是什么。只带个狱警去，屁用没有：他认识路，但他不知道囚犯那边真正在发生什么。我大概是唯一一个回到自己服刑过的监狱、在「释放前课程」上讲过话的囚犯，那次经历不可思议。我和典狱长、队长一起走在监狱大院里，我心想：这也太魔幻了。我在里面随便走？不——监狱里的行动是管制的，你不能想去哪儿就去哪儿。他们有所谓的「十分钟移动」（ten minute move），广播喊：「呃，十分钟移动，十分钟移动。」一点钟喊十分钟移动，你有十分钟离开自己住的单元，去你要去的地方。移动窗口一关，整个监狱就锁死了。去了大院，行，那就在大院待着，下个移动时段之前出不来；去了图书馆，就出不了那片；去了教学区或者任何地方，都出不了那个区域……

Until the next move, these are called controlled moves, and they're they're done for high security prisons. So that prison right now has moves. How do you move around a prison? 【译】……直到下一次移动。这叫「管制移动」（controlled move），高设防监狱都这么管。那所监狱现在就是移动制。——在监狱里怎么行动呢？——你要是不知道该去哪儿，在里面就寸步难行：里头怎么布局、小卖部（commissary）怎么运作（都得门清）。你知道这些监狱在赚多少钱吗？我出狱时都快气疯了：一台晶体管收音机，监狱里卖我100美元；出了狱，一模一样的收音机，外面卖9美元99美分。这怎么讲得通？因为他们就是在宰人，而且他们没意识到，被宰的是那些拼命凑钱送进来的家属。你知道打个电话多少钱吗？每月跟亲人通话只有300分钟——想想这点时间算什么。就这300分钟，15分钟一通的电话要3美元75美分。算算这笔钱——其实不是你花的，是你家人掏给你的。这到底伤害的是谁？我能修这个系统的地方太多了。要治暴力，第一招还是组队：一支有权进任何监狱的队伍。我不会一上来就开除典狱长——也许他真不知情。比如我带队进了某所监狱，查出里面有虐待：不放风、这事没做、那事没做，问题一堆，对吧？然后我把报告交给典狱长：把这些问题改了，我们还会再来复查。「有什么问题吗，典狱长？你们找了谁谈？情况怎么样？」——无可奉告。我们不告诉他什么时候回来。可能两个月，可能六个月，说不好。——我喜欢这个思路，让他们接受问责。我太喜欢了。现在没人这么干。你向谁推销过这个方案吗？——最近嘛……没有。我有一个切实可行的计划……

[11160s - 11280s]

For also for recidivism to lower recidivism, which is the rate of people coming back to prison, because almost almost two thirds go back to prison. He would talk to me through the shoot and say, "You know, we talk about God, we talk about life, we talk about a lot of things." And he kind of put me in a a better mindset for the next upcoming whatever. And he doesn't know it, but those little talks literally.

【译】（这个方案）也是冲着累犯去的——降低累犯率，也就是人重返监狱的比率，因为差不多三分之二的人会再进去，大约60%到65%吧，具体看你从哪一级监狱系统出来。所以除了改法律，还有别的办法：真有办法拿下一所监狱，止住暴力、止住虐待——因为施虐的通常只是一小撮狱警。是啊，他们手里有了权，而那种人打一开始就不配坐在那个位置上，就是拿破仑情结（Napoleon complex，矮子综合征）。他们能造成巨大的伤害，一个狱警就能造成巨大的伤害。——我的天。其他狱警也一样——警察也一样。我做警察培训项目，我了解：警察也是这个路数，他们……也会互相攻击。——你是说？这么说吧：人们跟我讲：「拉里，又不是所有警察都这样。」我说：「我问你个问题：如果我开了家公司、雇了一百个员工，我会不知道谁是捣蛋的吗？当然会知道，我迟早摸清他们是谁，然后开除。」一百个狱警里，其他那些人难道不知道谁是混子、谁在虐待囚犯、谁态度恶劣、谁在动手打人？你以为他们不知道？他们门儿清。——知道归知道，可你觉得他们会管吗？——他们不管，原因在这儿。有一个狱警救过我的命。他是个高大的黑人，我真希望知道他的名字，我要送他去度假。我在「洞」里发疯的时候干过各种事，闹到牢房都淹了水。我被打了太多回，以至于我觉得挨打才让我感觉自己活着。他以前常在夜里来到我的牢房——他上午夜班。他会打开外面的铁门，打开那个送饭口（chute），隔着送饭口跟我说话：「知道吗，我们聊上帝，聊人生，聊很多东西。」他总能让我在接下来不管什么日子里，心态好那么一点。他自己并不知道，但那些小小的交谈……

[11280s - 11400s]

Saved my life from going crazy, and it—the compassion he had, he, you know—and I, I used to get mad, Lex. I used to get mad. Why you know this is going on? You're telling me at night. Why don't you do something? It took me until I got out of prison to realize that man is trying to raise a family, feed his own family. He can't put himself in danger in that place because it is a violent place, and if he don't get the backing from his guard, something can happen, or he can be put on. So it took me the mature, mature part of me to realize. Wait a minute. He's doing that, and he's doing what he can instead of just, fuck, Lawton. Like you said, you're beaten, essentially tortured. What gave you strength through that? Mental strength. What made me survive more than anything was I had two young kids, and I just wanted to get out and see them. You know, I really feel there's so much to do. I love to help people, and now you got to remember, I got out of prison at 46 years old. I went away at 34, 46. So now I get a phone call talking to people doing good, having fun, meeting friends again. You know, starting to adapt a little bit to society. I get a phone call from a lady named Jean Bandish. She says, "Mr. Lawton, Judge Rymer would like to see you. I ain't seeing no judge. She goes, 'No, she just wants to hear about certain things.' And I go, 'Listen, do you have a warrant? Do you have a subpoena? Because I'm not coming to see no judge. I know the law. So no, she goes, 'I think you don't understand. She wants to hear that she heard you talk about kids. Still scared, thinking, 'What am I doing wrong? What do they want me for? I don't trust the system. And I agreed to do it, and I don't even know how to do anything. So I, I have my nephew help me. Since passed, nephew helped me with a PowerPoint. But the power—I didn't know what a PowerPoint at the time was. So we make.

【译】……救了我，没让我彻底疯掉。还有他那份慈悲。莱克斯，我以前会生气，真的会生气：「这些事明明在发生，你夜里都跟我讲了，你为什么不做点什么？」直到出狱以后我才明白：那个人要养家、要喂饱自己的一家人。在那样的地方，他不能把自己置于险境——那是个暴力之地。要是得不到其他狱警的撑腰，随时可能出事，或者被人记上一笔。所以我得靠心里成熟的那部分才想明白：等等，他已经在做他能做的事了，而不是一句「管他呢，劳顿爱咋咋地」。——就像你说的，你被殴打，实质上是被折磨。是什么支撑你挺过来？——意志力。真正让我撑下来的，是我有两个年幼的孩子，我一心想出去见他们。我真心觉得还有太多事要做，我热爱帮助别人。你要记得，我46岁才出狱——34岁进去，46岁出来。我出狱后跟人打电话、做好事、找乐子、和老友重逢，慢慢重新适应社会。然后我接到一个电话，一位名叫 Jean Bandish 的女士打来的。她说：「劳顿先生，Judge Rymer 想见您。」「我什么法官也不见。」她说：「不，她只是想听听某些事情。」我说：「听着，你们有逮捕令吗？有传票吗？没有我就不去见什么法官，我懂法。」她说：「我想您没明白，她听说了您给孩子们讲课的事，想听您讲讲。」我还是害怕，心想：「我做错什么了？他们找我干什么？」我不信任这个系统。但我还是答应了，可我什么都不会做，就让我侄子帮我——他如今已经去世了——侄子帮我做 PowerPoint。那时候我连 PowerPoint 是什么都不知道。于是我们就做……

[11400s - 11520s]

Take ten slides, Lex, about a PowerPoint, and as we're doing the PowerPoint, just slides of what I talk to the kids about. I go in on a Friday. I go into the judge's chambers. Now we find out years later, I'm not even supposed to be back there. I'm on paper and everything. Supposed to have security with me and all that kind of stuff. Now I go to dinner with a lot of my friends, so we sit in there. I walk in and there's two other people before them. Me, I'm sitting there. All right, Mr. Lawton, how you doing? Thank you for coming. Can you show us what you do? You know, they help me put the projector. I didn't know anything how to do that. I put it in the thing. I had a laptop and put the projector. I tell them what I do. Thank you, Mr. Lawton. Would you like to stay for the rest of the meeting? No, I'm out of here. And I literally want to get out of here. You know, I felt bad. I mean, you know, it's it's a weird feeling. I do that. I leave. It's a Friday Monday morning. I get a phone call from Gene Band. He says, "Hey, Mr. Lawton, I have to give you a heads up. Judge Ryman sentenced two people to your program. What fucking program? I have a program. From there, I developed the program. Now we have a 90 percent success rate in keeping kids out of jail. We have a 70 percent success rate by changed attitudes from the parents. We have a 43 percent success rate in increased education, better grades, and a 31 percent increase in attendance. And I didn't do that survey. Florida Eastern Florida State College did that. They asked for the data, people, and they did all the calling. They have all the graphs, all that stuff. And you would know about that anyway. They do this data, and I ended up getting recognized on the floor of Congress and and.

【译】……做了十页幻灯片，莱克斯。内容就是我平时跟孩子们讲的那些东西。周五那天我去了，走进法官的办公室。多年以后我们才知道，我按规矩根本不该出现在那种地方——我还在监管期（on paper），照规定得有安保跟着，有一堆要求。那阵子我常和一大帮朋友去吃晚饭，就这么顺道坐进去了。我走进去，前面还有两个人，然后是我，我就坐在那儿。「好了，劳顿先生，你好吗？谢谢你过来。能给我们展示一下你做的事情吗？」他们帮我弄投影仪——那些我一概不会。我把线插进机器，连上笔记本电脑和投影仪，给他们讲我做的事。「谢谢你，劳顿先生。你愿意留下来参加完剩下的会议吗？」「不了，我得走了。」我是真的一刻都不想多待。说实话我心里还有点过意不去，那种感觉很怪。就这样，我走了。周五过完，周一早上，我接到 Gene Band 打来的电话：「嘿，劳顿先生，我得提前跟您打个招呼：Judge Ryman 判了两个人上您的项目。」——什么他妈项目？我还有个项目？从那以后，我就把这个项目做了起来。现在，我们防止孩子入狱的成功率是90%；改变家长态度的成功率是70%；教育提升、成绩改善的成功率是43%；出勤率提升31%。这个评估不是我做的，是东佛罗里达州立学院（Eastern Florida State College）做的：他们要走了数据，做了全部回访电话，图表什么都有，反正都有据可查。就这样，我最后还在国会议事厅上获得了表彰，还有……

[11520s - 11640s]

For helping police agencies, because I also have a program where I help integrate police and stop the us against them mentality police have with police and the communities have. You know, it's such an us against them. So the program I developed, I was doing myself and doing, and now it's gotten so big that we had to put it on video. And now kids get sentenced to the program; they have to complete the the thing, then they have to take a a test and survey that they watched everything, get a certificate of completion, and give it to the court. It's so powerful. If we had that program in Fort Lauderdale right here, where Cruz killed the kids from the high school, and I lived right near, not far from that high school, they would have had the police would have had a tool to try to intervene and help this kid before he went crazy because you know they had many interactions with that kid and they never gave him it. They didn't have anything. Even if it didn't work, they could say they tried. We tried this program, that program. We gave it to him. We tried to help the parent, and I mean, police have a tough enough job, obviously, and to pick something out. But when you have that many interactions with somebody, and we even put it where it can be used for detention. Kid does something stupid in school; he has to go there. He has to watch the program. He has to pass the program, and then you can see because I I do a whole survey, and I I show you thousands, and you can see the mindset. And if you saw a kid that said, you know, I hate the world anyway, I might as well just shoot someone. Boy, red flag can go off. You can then get counseling into this kid, find out really where his head's at. There's ways to do things again with young people, and mostly because they they respect me. I'm not one of the the system. I wouldn't rat, so my credibility is right. I'm not a former drug addict, so it's not. I'm not going to do drugs. He was just a drug addict and cleaned his life out. Everybody wants to make money. Everybody.

【译】……因为帮了警察机构。我还做了一个项目，帮警察融入社区，消除警察跟警察之间、警察跟社区之间那种「我们对阵他们」的心态。那种对立太重了。这个项目起初是我一个人在做，后来越做越大，不得不拍成视频。现在孩子们会被法院判来上这个项目：必须完整学完，然后参加考试和问卷，证明全部看过，拿到结业证书，交回法院。这项目威力很大。要是当时在劳德代尔堡——就是 Cruz 枪杀高中学生的那地方，我住得离那所高中很近——就有这个项目，警察手里就多了一件工具，能在这个孩子崩溃之前介入、帮他。要知道，警察跟那个孩子打过很多次交道，却从来没给过他这样的东西，因为他们手里什么都没有。就算没用，他们至少能说试过了：试了这个项目、那个项目，给他做了干预，也帮过家长。警察的工作本来就够难了，要从中识别征兆更是难上加难。但既然跟一个人打了那么多交道……我们还设计成可以用于惩戒留置（detention）：孩子在学校干了蠢事，就必须去那里看这个项目，还得通过考核。然后你就能看到——我做了完整的问卷调研，能给你看成千上万份，你能看到孩子的心态。要是你看到一个孩子写「反正我恨这个世界，干脆开枪打人算了」——好家伙，红灯立刻亮起，你就能马上给这孩子安排心理辅导，弄清他脑子里到底在想什么。跟年轻人打交道是有方法的，主要因为他们尊重我：我不是体制里的人；我这辈子没告过密（rat），所以信誉立得住；我也不是什么前瘾君子——不是那种「他以前是瘾君子、后来把日子过回来」的路数……

[11640s - 11760s]

He wants to be somebody, and then so you take all those positives and you bring them to a kid. And I, I have video reviews from kids that make me cry because they, you know, some of them have rough lives. And when you hear how they, this one kid, man, it's so powerful, starts crying. I'm like, I mean, I, I wanted a hug. I did actually, and he's young, maybe 19, 20. Because we found out my program is not just for teenagers; it's for kids 25. Kids, we all know, and you'll know this: a young man's brain doesn't mature till 25. A female about 23. Not my numbers, psychology numbers, and it's true. So, and you know, anybody making bad choices needs a wake-up call. What could happen to them if they keep making bad choices? And then that program actually works with cards with police agencies. So they actually carry a video card. The card is actually the video. It's like a credit card. It's got a QR code with a code for the police. The police chief opens the message, says, "We'd like you to watch this video. It might help your child or child." The kids watch it now, and the police chief closes it by saying, "We hope this helped you. If you have any problems, please contact us." Each cop carries them, ten of them in their pocket. And you know, we we're going to push more of that. That's where the government loved. I mean, I used to work with the Weed and Seed program. That's a government agency. Which again, funding they take the funding away. Weed and Seed program was a program that the government gave, and they would go to a neighborhood and weed out the bad with the U.S. attorney. This is federal now. They'd take the U.S. attorney in that area. They'd get the drugs off the streets, get the gangs out of it, and then seed it with programs like mine. So it was Weed and Seed program. It was a very successful program. Again, funding cut that, which drives me crazy when you have a program like that with success rate.

【译】（那样的孩子）他是想成为个人物的。你就把这些正能量全部汇聚起来，带给一个孩子。我还留着孩子们的视频感言，看得我落泪——有些孩子的日子过得很苦。听他们讲的时候……有个孩子，天哪，冲击力特别强，他哭了起来。我当时就想抱抱他——我真抱了。他还那么年轻，大概19、20岁。后来我们发现，我的项目不只适合十几岁的少年，25岁以下都适用。众所周知——你也知道——年轻男性的大脑要到25岁才发育成熟，女性大约23岁。这不是我编的数字，是心理学的数字，而且是真的。所以，任何正在做糟糕选择的人，都需要一记警钟：再这样错下去，会有什么下场？这个项目现在还和警察机构合作做卡片：警察随身带着一种视频卡——卡片本身就是视频，像一张信用卡，上面有二维码（QR code）和警察专用的验证码。警察局长打开留言：「希望您观看这段视频，它也许能帮到您的孩子。」孩子看完，局长在片尾说：「希望这对你有帮助。如果遇到任何问题，请联系我们。」每个警察口袋里都装着，一装十张。我们打算进一步推广，政府就吃这一套。我以前还跟 Weed and Seed（「先铲除后扶植」）项目合作过，那是政府的项目。结果呢，经费又被砍了。Weed and Seed 是政府办的项目：他们进驻一个社区，会同联邦检察官（U.S. attorney）——这就到联邦层面了——把当地的联邦检察官也拉进来，把毒品清出街道，把帮派赶出去，然后再用像我这样的项目去「播种」。所以叫 Weed and Seed（除草与播种）。那是个非常成功的项目——又一次，经费被砍。一个有实效的成功项目落得这个下场，真把我气疯了。

[11760s - 11880s]

And I used to go speak at the things, and my program was vetted by the federal government, and they used it. And and then I spoke in Texas, actually. In Texas, they have the International Association of Police Chiefs headquarters, and it's I spoke there to the executive board, actually, because there's one of my mentors in the policing who gave me a badge, and that's weird because I'm not I'm not a supporter of most police. Most police who are screw ups don't deserve, and I'm not going to lose my reputation because some jerk off police chief wants to make me and think he's great. No, there's a police department in St. Louis in Lake St. Louis, unbelievable guy named Mike Force to this day we're friends. He's retired. Narducci, I'm still an honorary police there. I got the card, all works. Maybe get me out of a ticket. I don't know. I don't even use it. I don't even carry it. But they're so positive because they really think about how to help people, and their first thing is not arrest. It's help. He says anybody starts telling you arrest, they don't understand the game. It's how can we help you? You know, when I teach police academies, and I did in St. Louis, is the first thing a cop should do when they pull somebody over is not say license or registration. How about going up to the guy and saying, "Excuse me, sir, are you okay? " You know, you ran that you ran that stop sign, and I'm not even going to give you a ticket. I just want to make sure you're all right because people get hit broadside, and a lot of people can get killed. Are you all right? Everything good? Yes, I'm all right. And what community positive? That guy goes home, tells everybody in the world how cool the cop is, instead of license registration. You know what you did? You blew the sign. Who the fuck are you, kid? You know, especially as a 22 year old cop, and I'm a 60 year old man. I want to strangle him. And then you have a battery, but but they have to understand they're part of a community, not.

【译】我以前常去各种场合演讲，我的项目通过了联邦政府的审查，他们也在用。后来我还去德州演讲——国际警察局长协会（IACP）的总部就在德州，我在那儿给他们的执行委员会讲过。因为我警界有位恩师，给了我一枚警徽。这事挺怪的，因为我并不支持大多数警察——大多数混账警察不配（这身皮）。我也不会因为哪个蠢货警察局长想拿我装点门面、显得自己了不起，就赔上自己的名声。不过——圣路易斯那边的圣路易斯湖市（Lake St. Louis）有个警察局，局长叫 Mike Force，人好得难以置信，到今天我们还是朋友，他退休了。还有纳杜奇（Narducci）那儿，我至今是他们的名誉警察。我有那张卡，都有效，也许能让我免张罚单？谁知道呢。我根本不用，连随身都不带。但那家警局风气特别正，因为他们真心琢磨怎么帮人，第一反应不是抓人，是帮忙。他说，谁要一开口就是「抓人」，谁就不懂这一行；真正的问法是：我们怎么能帮到你？我在警校讲课——在圣路易斯讲过——警察拦停车辆之后，第一句话不该是「驾照、行驶证」。为什么不上前问一句：「先生您好，您还好吗？您刚才那个停车牌没停——罚单我就不开了，我只想确认您没事。那种路口经常侧面撞车，会出人命的。您还好吧？一切正常吗？」「嗯，我没事。」这对社区多好？那人回到家，逢人就说那个警察有多酷。而不是「驾照！行驶证！你知道你干了什么吗？你闯了停车牌！你算老几，小子？」——尤其对方是个22岁的小警察，我都60岁的人了，我真想掐死他。然后就闹出袭击（battery）案。但他们必须明白：自己是社区的一分子，而不是……

[11880s - 12000s]

Just us against them. We're the cops. We got the gun. And those stories travel. The good and the bad stories travel. So you got to put the good, the good ones. One hundred percent. And you know, you get enough. And that all happened because a friend of mine was seventy eight since passed, but seventy eight, and he gets stopped. Had four people in the car going to dinner. He rolled a stop sign, and a cop comes up. You rolled it. He told the story to everybody I know. You rolled it. What? Like you rolled the sign. Give me your license. Then he came back and threw the. He said, "I can't give you a ticket because I got a call, but don't roll it." He told that story to every person I know, and instead of sorry having all right, elderly man, nice, very successful guy, just stupid. What advice would you give to young people? Uh, make sure your word is good. Uh, that's probably the number one thing I think. I think, and and it starts early. If you say something, do it, and that doesn't mean you can't have a mistake. And people will know you have mistakes. Own up to them. Don't think life's over. You know, I also tell people this, and this I think is very important. A lot of young people come up to me at thirty years old. I can't do it. I can't make it. I, you know, I messed up. I said, "Hold on, buddy." I was forty six years old with a record that you don't have, and I had to get out of prison and start over. How do I do it? Perseverance, work hard, don't give up. You know, the people who give up either are looking for pity. I don't believe in pity. I believe in working hard. I think people who do work hard will get a hand up by a lot of people because people like to see that, and that to me is the biggest. Your life's never, and I mean that for older people.

【译】……不是「我们对阵他们」「我们是警察，我们有枪」。这些故事会传开，好的坏的都会传。所以你得尽量留下好故事。——百分之百。再说一个：我一位朋友，78岁——如今已经去世了——78岁那年，车上带着四个人去吃晚饭，过停车牌没停死（rolling stop），警察走上来说：「你刚才溜牌了。」这事他后来讲给了我认识的每一个人听。「你溜牌了。」「什么？」「就是你过停车牌没停。」「出示你的驾照。」然后警察回来，把驾照递还给他，说：「罚单本该开的，但我接了个出警，这次就算了，别再溜牌了。」他把这个故事讲给了我认识的每一个人。而不是那种（糟糕的收场）——本可以说一声「没事了，您慢走」。那是位老人，和善、非常成功的人士，（粗暴对待）纯属愚蠢。——你会给年轻人什么建议？——嗯，确保你的承诺算数（your word is good）。我想这大概是第一位的，而且要趁早：说到就要做到。这不代表你不能犯错——人们早晚会知道你犯了错，那就坦然承认，别觉得人生就此完蛋。我还常跟人说一句话，我觉得非常重要。很多30岁的年轻人来找我：「我不行了，我做不成了，我搞砸了。」我说：「打住，伙计。我46岁，背着你没有的前科，刚出监狱，一切从头开始。我是怎么做的？坚持，拼命干，绝不放弃。」那些放弃的人，多半是在等人可怜。我不信可怜这一套，我只信埋头苦干。我相信真正努力的人，会有很多人愿意拉一把，因为人们乐见这种劲头。对我来说，这是最要紧的。你的人生永远不会（完蛋）——这话对年纪更大的人同样算数。

[12000s - 12074s]

Again, forty-six years old. You're getting out of prison. Life's not over. Find out what you love. I love what I do. I, as you can tell, I do. I mean, I love people, and I love to talk to young people. You know, Nick working with me, and his energy is matches mine, and that's pretty tough. I mean, I just have a lot of energy. Listen, when when it's all over, lights are out. I have plenty of time to rest. You know, I agree. So make sure your word is good, and and then work your ass off. Never quit. Never quit. Larry, you're you're an incredible human being. Thank you for having me. I really appreciate that. You know, you better be a character in a Grand Theft Auto game. I'll put my vote in. Thank you, Larry. Thank you very much. Thanks for listening to this conversation with Larry Lawton. To support this podcast, please check out our sponsors in the description, where you can also find links to contact me, ask questions, give feedback, and so on. Thank you for listening, and hope to see you next time.

【译】再说一遍：46岁，刚出狱，人生没有结束。找到你热爱的事。我热爱我做的事——你看得出来，我确实热爱。我热爱人，热爱跟年轻人交谈。尼克（Nick）跟我一起干，他的精力能跟我对上——这可不容易，因为我精力太旺盛了。听着，等一切落幕、灯光熄灭，我有的是时间休息。——我同意。所以：确保你的承诺算数，然后拼命去干。永不放弃。永不放弃。——拉里，你是个了不起的人。——谢谢你的邀请，我真的很感激。——还有，你最好能成为《侠盗猎车手》（Grand Theft Auto）里的角色，我会投上一票。——谢谢你，拉里。——多谢了。——感谢收听本期与拉里·劳顿（Larry Lawton）的对话。想支持本播客，请查看节目简介中的赞助商信息；那里也有联系我的链接，可以提问、提意见等。感谢收听，我们下期再见。
